# PvP Update — Potential Changes

## Post 124491 by Akalix (Turtle WoW Team) [STAFF] — 2025-03-30T19:55:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124491#p124491 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

**Greetings!**

Over time as we have gone further down the path of **Vanilla+** and departed from 1:1 vanilla parity, we've made significant changes to all aspects of the game. With this in mind, there are a handful of potential new features and design choices we would like feedback on before implementing. Please note that we reserve the right to make unpopular decisions if we think they will be beneficial to the overall health of the network. As of now, we are not confirming any of these changes, only confirming that we plan to do **something**.

 **Honor Rework**

The current implementation of **Honor** is a slightly more forgiving version of the original vanilla system, where the optimal way to rank up is not improving your gameplay, it's increasing how much time you spend grinding honor. The system, especially at the higher levels, requires an unsustainable amount of queue spamming. There are numerous ways we could change this system—and we would like to know what changes the community would like to see, including if people would rather see no changes at all.

 **Rated PvP**

 **Rated PvP** is a commonly requested feature, as the current Honor system doesn't serve as a true skill indicator, rather a indication of time played. At our current population, we **do not** plan to add a rated Battleground feature, as we do not have the PvP population to support it. However, we are considering adding **Rated Arena Teams** and/or a **Solo Shuffle**-esq queue. These appeal to two different types of players, with arena teams being premade and coordinating their play, and a solo shuffle queue perfect for players who would rather log on and "just go" rather than wait for a team.

 **PvP Itemization Update**

Currently because there's limited ways to itemize specifically for PvP, PvP encounters are very bursty, it's kill or be killed.

This was "solved" after vanilla by introducing full item sets with Resilience on them.

We would like the community's input on ways we could limit this burst-paced gameplay and have already written out a small list of things we think might work:
- **PvP Set Bonuses** which would give strong effects for PvP combat.

- **PvP (instanced) Item Buffs** akin to retail's current approach, which buffs PvP items significantly when in PvP.

- **Resilience** or a similar system, which provides stats specifically beneficial to PvP from PvP gear.
With this, we would much prefer to change the balance of PvP via itemization, rather than bloating abilities with pvp-exclusive conditionals.

 **Closing**

In closing, I would like to reiterate that none of these changes are directly confirmed—only a handful of concepts that we are open to pursuing if our community is interested in them. Please let us know your feedback on these ideas, as we are actively looking for our community's opinions.

## Post 124528 by Phoenixphire (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-03-30T23:50:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124528#p124528 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Obviously burst is going to be an issue with the way a lot of the classes currently work and the ever scaling nature of vanilla and the way the stats work so implementing more stam, armor, or spell resistances to PVP armor is a possible help. Also using an resilience system of some kind for pvp seems like an inevitable change because as we have seen with both SoD and with Twow, classic+ always seems to have burst problems in pvp without heavy rebalancing of pvp gear. I am going to stick to content and system suggestions in this reply rather than go into balance issues with classes.

My suggestions are this (not going into class balance atm):

Overall I think there needs to be more pvp activities and ways to gain gear from pvp besides just BGs and Honor, these could be open world, pvp factions in a zone that people can join and fight others to gain rep and or pvp themed world bosses that flag you when fighting and have some incentive to defend would be cool along with the upcoming faction leader loot. If you are not gonna add stuff like this... then I think at the very least new BGs, new pvp gear and or new pvp ranks past 14 with unique rewards would be a cool thing to see.

I 100% think if you decide to make ranking easier that you should add further ranks with unique rewards. I think it would help make people who got rank 14 when it was hard feel less crappy that others can get it easier if they now have new ranks and cool unique rewards to shoot for. Also it would create more content for pvpers since there will be more new raids, dungeons etc coming but once one hits rank 14 (ESPECIALLY if its made easier to get) pvp loses a lot of its meaning while pve gets new raids and new cool stuff.

I personally believe going small scale for pvp like arenas is a bad idea as I (personal opinion alert) think larger scale pvp obscures balance issues better than small scale which is better for a server more focused on pve balance.

Another cool option could be Race based daily or weekly pvp objectives. Example:

A race could have a "pvp faction" Like I am an undead and our rep is called the Deathstalkers, there is a Deathstalker area in Undercity that gives daily and weekly quests for undead only. You could also make it so while you have these quests in your log you are flagged for pvp to prevent issues with non warmode people. These could range from killing Humans in AB, killing a few people in Plague Lands or Going to an enemy factions area (Lets say the gnomes) and dropping a plague barrel on their stuff. Maybe even a really hard one like killing a faction leader if you can manage it, or add new pvp themed 5man difficulty bosses around the world could be the objective of one of these quests.(The idea is the opposite faction would have some mirrors or similar versions of these quests so that people bump into other pvpers trying to do these quests creating pvp) Completing these could give rep, crafting materials, and you could use this rep to get awesome race themed pvp gear such as undead themed pvp style gear or Nightelf themed pvp style gear. This adds 3 things.

 1. More pvp content that gets people out in the open world since you folks already have added some cool new instanced pvp.

 2. Some variety and replayability you can use it to send people to zone they may not visit as often, and have lower level players seeing higher level players out killing each other would add some extra immersion. As well as breaks up the normal only bgs grind a little.

 3. Really cool way to earn new pvp items as well as lots of RP value as it will be race themed so people who wanna collect the cool race themed armor or guilds that RP with only 1 race will have a great time doing these.

A smaller scale version of this could be done in a specific zone by just having players do pvp weekly or daily quests or kill people in that zone. For example Burning Steppes you could have the Alliance work with some renegade Dark Iron Dwarves and the Horde work with that group of Blackrock Orcs that are friendly and get cool pvp rewards, recipes, crafting materials etc. (This could be a great thing to do in the upcoming Eversong Wastes Horde gets trolls and Ally get Highelves and duke it out in the zone for pvp rewards)

Either way, beyond just balance which plenty of people will harp on in these replies. I think for PVP to survive and or thrive, it needs new content beyond the rank 14 and BG grind and I think getting people out into Turtle Wow's ever expanding open world or at the very least adding more content to the rank 14+ and BG grind.

If you don't know what the system is, honor is capped at 500k per week and you no longer decay at all. In order to rank up you have to hit specific honor milestones that increase by rank. ## Post 124565 by Lomikare1 — 2025-03-31T06:03:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124565#p124565 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

**Advantages and Disadvantages of PvP on Classic / Turtle WoW**

 **ADVANTAGES**
 - There is no separation between PvP and PvE. If someone has nice raid equipment, they are also strong in battlegrounds.

 - Strong class identity and the principle of rock, paper, scissors. Each class has its strengths and weaknesses, leading to situations where certain matchups are easier for one class and harder for the other.

 - The epicness of Rank 14. While it is "grindy gameplay," it doesn't penalize inactivity and preserves the original epic feeling.

 **DISADVANTAGES**
 - There are no PvP rewards worth pursuing. The current Rank 14 gear is much weaker than Naxx gear. It isn't worth going for it unless you care about the "cool transmog."

 - PvP is too "burst-heavy" because classes deal higher damage. This is especially evident with casters in PvP.

 - The reputation rewards with individual PvP factions are weak and uninteresting. Gaining reputation takes effort, and it would be better if the items were either cool or useful. They don’t need to be overpowered, just good to have.

 - There is no such thing as "Rated PvP." PvP players, therefore, have no way to showcase their "skill"—they can only show "grind."

 **WHAT TO DO?**
 - Keep the honor system and individual PvP ranks, but add Rated arenas where ranking is calculated. The ranking would show how good a team is in the arena, something players want. It could also influence honor gain through a coefficient (the better your ranking, the more honor you gain, and the faster you progress in rank).

 - Address the "burst-heavy" meta by reducing damage classes deal in PvP. However, I wouldn’t go the route of Resistance, as it separates PvP from PvE and divides the community, which would be a shame. Instead, I recommend using existing game mechanics, particularly Magic Resistance and Armor.

 - PvP gear needs an update, and simply increasing stats isn't meaningful (as raids will always surpass it, and more raids will be added). I would add increased Armor and significant bonuses to Magic Resistances to each piece of the PvP set (e.g., +40 Fire Resistance on helmets, +40 Frost Resistance on shoulders). This would ensure classes deal less damage to each other. This gear would also be useful for PvE players, as Magic Resistances are essential for certain encounters.

 - Add Holy Resistance among the schools of Magic Resistances.

 - Add useful and interesting trinkets and gear to the PvP factions that players gain reputation with. Not overpowered, but items that make life easier and are simply "nice to have" (e.g., a unique 24-slot bag, etc.).

I hope you will enjoy my sugestions and find them usefull. Wish you a great day and thanks for your hard work! O:)

*Last edited by Lomikare1 on Mon Mar 31, 2025 6:48 pm, edited 1 time in total.*

At our current population, we **do not** plan to add a rated Battleground feature, as we do not have the PvP population to support it. These appeal to two different types of players, with arena teams being premade and coordinating their play, and a solo shuffle queue perfect for players who would rather log on and "just go" rather than wait for a team.

Sounds great and that will draw new players in for sure. Just make the rewards worth it. The current BR itemization is just weird for many classes.

> **Akalix wrote: Sun Mar 30, 2025 7:55 pm**
> **PvP Itemization Update**
>
>  Currently because there's limited ways to itemize specifically for PvP, PvP encounters are very bursty, it's kill or be killed.
>
>  This was "solved" after vanilla by introducing full item sets with Resilience on them.
>
>  We would like the community's input on ways we could limit this burst-paced gameplay and have already written out a small list of things we think might work:
> - **PvP Set Bonuses** which would give strong effects for PvP combat.
>
> - **PvP (instanced) Item Buffs** akin to retail's current approach, which buffs PvP items significantly when in PvP.
>
> - **Resilience** or a similar system, which provides stats specifically beneficial to PvP from PvP gear.
>   With this, we would much prefer to change the balance of PvP via itemization, rather than bloating abilities with pvp-exclusive conditionals.

Well as has been commented since November, the 17.2 patch and the new tier sets increased the burstiness and powercreep in pvp significantly. Compare the new t3 sets with, for example, the old t2.5 for warriors and shamans which were considered the 'endgame' pvp sets in vanilla. And you've created whole new monsters in for example the disc priests. So ultimately you will need to revisit CC2 if you want to address the burstiness. Solving it with gear will only lead to further stat inflation.

That said, the pvp gear needs to be buffed for sure. Bringing the current sets to endgame power level, and increasing the diversity of sets (look at the shaman set for example) so that you have 2-3 sets per class, is a good start.
I think the crit dmg reduction bonuses you have been working with looks good, but too often it exists in competition with other stats (i.e items have poor stats otherwise) which makes it a second choice.

I would also like to add:
- BGs: You need to fix the queue system so that people can easily play together. I think this is best done by raising the min. amount of players needed to start the game. 5v5 ABs are neither balanced or fun, raise it to min. 10v10. or preferably 12v.12. Same with WSGs - class imbalances become more visible with fewer players. You need to have 10v10 or min 8v8 starting amount of players. Joining games halfway through when they are often already decided is unbalanced and boring. Yes, this will also facilitate 'premading' which is what you want if you want to have an active pvp community. The current uncertainty of whether you will end up in the same BG together as your mates or not kills the incentive to que.

- It would be great if you can add TBC's Eye of the Storm as it is a tried and tested BG concept. Would only need slight re-skinning to fit with the Caverns of Time fluff.

- Moreover, particularly on Tel'abim we would like some changes to incentivise world pvp. Faction leader loot and towers have been repeatedly requested since server opened. I would also like to raise attention to a suggestion I made a few months ago about a wpvp 'challenge': [viewtopic.php?p=116975#p116975](https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=116975#p116975)

## Post 124599 by Wolf of Rage (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-03-31T08:54:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124599#p124599 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

No Cross-Faction BGs or whatever nonsense.

Integrate PvP-sided rebalances that take effect in PvP Instances (BGs and so on) made at various later points of WoW -- like v3.3.2 in WotLK with Healing reduced in BGs, Arenas, Wintergrasp etc.

Bring back some sort of punishments for PvP griefers, because their current lack indirectly supports such players while as a domino effect, discouraging those just trying out PvP, or are newcomers.

Redesign the Honor climb -- cap the Honor you can get, while also instating some sort of minimum. The first would combat ruthless clique-like Honor farmers that are still rampant. The second would make it so that not just every random average Joe could have an easy time, thus get filtered out -- to prevent oversaturation and everyone basically having an easy entry and then upkeep within the Rank System. The theory with having both from my perspective would be to even the playing field while neither making it too hard, nor too easy -- just consistent.

Resilience in its literal form would not translate well in this iteration of the game, even with custom Items (unless burst just becomes so absurd well past even Vanilla's standards, that Resilience would then end up indeed becoming good). It was needed and well received due to the fact that with later phases of the game's lifespan, numbers would get so jacked, that something was necessary to make it fair and not like you're playing some MMORPG variant of Diablo II PvP where you can die offscreen to something in the blink of an eye. Rather, a spiritual successor -- percentage-based, found on some Items and as Set Bonus breakpoints (but don't make them the sole Bonuses else the Sets themselves just become glorified Resilience Sets anyway). Need to be careful with how much can be gathered in total and how much each is tuned, because you don't want to result in, let's say, Holy Paladins being immortal with such Items as their new carry. This must also absolutely not have any effect in PvE -- basically such Items would be useless in PvE outside of any other Stats they may provide (like higher Stamina). Let's be honest and say that this worked in TBC and WotLK, but by Cataclysm (and strangely, it would be here as well), it just needed to be gone from affecting PvE.

If the above about "Resilience" doesn't work or rather, a fine-tune implementation method is not possible, then a different or at least complimentary method could be utilized: PvP-side modifiers which only ever occur when a Target is a Player or a Player Pet, automatically not working if the Target is instead a Mob. Anyone here who might have played Souls games -- especially and particularly Elden Ring -- may have seen PvP-only changes amongst their many changelogs. Those are to try to keep PvE from being affected by changes revolving around PvP, as well as try to have PvP be at least... less imbalanced, due to changes that primarily affect PvE. Such things would be of use here as well, because, let's face it -- the more we go on, with new heightened Stats, burstier metas, and a bunch of new things that are harder and more annoying to balance for both Environments, one side is just always going to be forced to bite the bullet (in which case, it's always PvP). For example, if the detected Target is recognized as a Player or a Player Pet, reduce Damage Dealt by some percentage, Healing Done by some percentage; the same applying to you as a Player Target recipient of a Damage or Healing Spell -- take less, be healed less, and so on. This would be so that PvE Items would not continue to be monstrous crutches carrying over from PvE straight into PvP, as well as help make the PvP meta less of a meme than how it already is (still, try not to make Holy Paladins more immortal than they already are with this as well).

## Post 124612 by Crake (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-03-31T09:48:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124612#p124612 | page 1 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

The two biggest offenders I think in pvp are crits and dots, thats generally pretty well established

The issue with crits, especially at much higher levels of itemization, is that most attacks end up almost being guaranteed crits, and the crits themselves do too much damage.

Potential solutions:
 - Set a hard crit cap against players
 - Reduce critical damage vs players (From 200% damage down to 150% or even 125%)
 - Have an internal cooldown on critical strikes against players
 - a stack -> crit system giving some form of control over when critical strikes occur, at the expense of the frequency of critical strikes. Might step on the toes of some abilities like cold blood that can guarantee a crit already

As for dots, I feel like the issue isn't so much their bursty nature, but rather that they are so much prolonged damage for often such little investment. An instant cast dot that many classes have no direct answer to outside of consumables, that can often deal upwards of half the hp of a character, or given some cases of high gear disparity, even one shot characters, I feel like the only reasonable solution is to cut down on the duration of dots, rather than their damage. It's fine to have a high dps dot if it only lasts 6 seconds instead of 18.

As for pvp itemization, instead of defensive boosts vs other players, the pvp gear can offset these reductions, meaning players can bring back some of their burstiness (though ideally not offeset them completely) at the expense of pve set bonuses, and glass canon itemization. If you want those full damage crits, and full duration dots, you can have them, but you're gonna be in gear that prioritizes stamina over offensive stats as a result.

## Post 124643 by Bjorn88 (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-03-31T11:38:00Z

TA pop is currently booming. Horde has plenty of pvpers, the problem mostly lies with Alliance not queing.

Further, a list of things currently impact TA BGs negatively:

- The que system. As mentioned in a previous post, starting BGs with 5v5 and letting players trickle in on a one by one basis is a very bad idea. It yields initially imbalanced games which are hard to recover from for latecoming players. Since you can practically only get in one at a time, it also prevents players from joining as a group and playing together, which is FUNDAMENTAL if you want a healthy pvp scene. The current system practically forces pugs and kills organized pvp. Increase starting amount of player to 10v10 (AB) and 8v8 (WSG).

## Post 124645 by Jackbonus — 2025-03-31T11:54:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124645#p124645 | page 2 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Who's to say the resilience has to be an end all fix all, and why can't it be a simple solution or doing both things?
I even think reworking the pvp gear is already happening as we speak.
I personally think adding a resilience stat would do wonders for the time to kill issue. On top of that I think the best pvp gear should follow along with addition of new PvE items and be perhaps 10-15% weaker but with other stats that is suited better for PvP and less useful for PvE.

I think it would be fair that someone who is kitted out in the best of the best pvp gear could join raids and still be decent on damage/healing, but with lots of room for improvement if they wanted to get the bis PvE gear. (They should oom faster and have a general lower output, but not be useless altogether)
Just like I think it's fair someone could join battlegrounds in bis, tier 3.5 and be a powerhouse on dmg, but then very fragile and a sort of easy target compared to people in PvP gear.

And then most optimally I'd see the endgame BIS items for PvP be a dynamic mix and match of both PvP and PvE gear dependent on how much resilience/survivability you feel your class or spec needs in the given situation. This could and would change weather you are playing arenas or battlegrounds for example.
This would leave a whole world of options for people to theorycraft, experiment, and mixmax in many different ways, and I believe this is indeed what so many of love to have the possibility to do.

On another topic, the battlegrounds and potentially rated arenas. First and foremost the arenas should be cross faction so you could face every type of team. Let the shamans and paladin debates finally be solved by having them face themselves!

And then a question, because I think probably the biggest challenge rated arenas are facing atm is to reach a critical mass of players so the meta becomes fluid and there will be good queues.
Would it be possible for cross server bgs to help solve this?
Let Tel abim and Nordenaar face eachother In bgs, and if latency doesn't get in the way perhaps the Latin server too?

Anyway this is just my 50 cent. So happy to see these topics are getting attention finally! As old-school tbc PvP has such a big place in my heart.

Godspeed turtledevs! I hope you manage to create something truly special here ![❤️](//cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/twitter/twemoji@latest/assets/svg/2764.svg)

## Post 124650 by Setris — 2025-03-31T12:17:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124650#p124650 | page 2 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Ladhihs wrote: Mon Mar 31, 2025 12:12 am**
> For the honor rework you should copy Blizzard's system from Era/SoD 1 for 1. Don't try to reinvent the wheel, don't try anything new or special. Just copy paste it into Turtle.
>  If you don't know what the system is, honor is capped at 500k per week and you no longer decay at all. In order to rank up you have to hit specific honor milestones that increase by rank. If you don't hit your next milestone then you stay exactly where you are in progress. This system is the best as it allows people to truly rank at their own leisure.

- Honor System: +1 for the ClassicEra honor system, where we can grind at our own pace and last ranks are somehow achievable. I have a life, and I can play 1 to 2 weeks at max, then take a rest for a month or so. This is representation of my grinding road into the Era Servers, with my busy life achieved for a couple of months, but played a total of 9 weeks:

Estimate 7-11k per hour with an average of 9k/hr.
Week 1: Rank 1 0% 33.75k honor ~ 3.75 hr
Week 2: Rank 4 60% 110k honor ~ 12.25 hr
Week 3: Rank 7 20% 256.25k honor ~ 28.5 hr
Week 4: Rank 9 60% 418.75k honor ~ 46.5 hr
Week 5: Rank 11 40% 500k honor ~ 55.5 hr
Week 6: Rank 12 64% 500k honor ~ 55.5 hr
Week 7: Rank 13 34% 420k honor ~ 46.5 hr
Week 8: Rank 13 68% 420k honor ~ 46.5 hr
Week 9: Rank 14 Earned.
 9k/hr is achievable if playing actively. Inactively drops to around 7k/hr. For the AV weekend, you can get 13- 15k/hr.

- Rated Content: I am against rated content. If I am playing competitive things, it is too stressful for me, and this is making me quit too soon. I am old already and have no energy for this. If you implement such a thing, please do not separate rewards from rated and standard BGs. Maybe purchasing Items with Honor again, but receiving additional from this new rated content for the people who want to grind faster. This is the charm of Classic Era PVP, where you can do it with your own leasure without forcing yourself to hurry or do content you do not like.

- Itemization: update of the current gear to be on PVE tiers level + additional ranks if new tiers are invented, like we expect tier 3.5, so a new rank 15 or filling of the current ranks with respective set gear pieces must be added for natural progression, like what we receive in PVE with tiers grinding.
 - PvP Set Bonuses: I see great potential in this idea.
 - Gear Scaling(item buffs/nerfs): is the worst thing ever invented in BLizard and that is what made me quit SoD after I fond there is some kind of retail scaling during my first BG there at lvl19, after the BG I just exit the game and uninstalled. With scaling progression feeling is somehow screwed and gearing element is killed. I am ready to die more in PVP if I am new or do some PVE content to gear up first.
 - Resilience: It could be a great idea, but this way you are separating PVE from PVP gear, and I am not sure how you can handle that much of an itemization development. Also, if I am mainly a PVP player, but with the current itemization, I can still go to experience PVE content, resilience is locking me to one kind of content. Also, I like how the game forces me to experience some PVE if I am low geared. If I have the option for the off pieces to be available from Dungeons not Raids would be the best thing ever I can think about.

## Post 124704 by Amptie — 2025-03-31T17:14:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124704#p124704 | page 2 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I would like to see some massive changes:

 - first of all X-Faction BGs have to be a thing, so this Paladin/Shaman whine fest comes to a stop, also grouping as a guild is not always possible because of factions (which is thematically right, but for the gameplay faction restricted BGs prevent people from playing)

 - secondly I would recommend introducing some kind of lifetime points, which can be obtained by turning in insignias from every battleground: 3xAB, 3xWSG and 3xAV in comination (important! so everyone has to play every battleground) for about 100 points and after lets say 30,000 points you'll reach rank 14. People who already have ranks could get awarded the mininum points to reach that rank

this would be still time consuming, but you have an incentive to win to get faster marks. This can prevent people from just turtling AVs and more concentrate on objectives within the BG

 - according to the burst damage problem I think a tweak in the resilience stat could do the thing. Maybe you could add reduce damage (overall) when in areas of BGs and Arena. So you have -3% damage and -3% crit damage when you are in a battleground. This would effectively be some kind of TBC resilience (not exactly but close) but it would only count in battlegrounds, not in open world (since its a PvE server, this should be fine).

just my ideas :)

> - secondly I would recommend introducing some kind of lifetime points, which can be obtained by turning in insignias from every battleground: 3xAB, 3xWSG and 3xAV in comination (important! so everyone has to play every battleground) for about 100 points and after lets say 30,000 points you'll reach rank 14. Some things already feel like breaching said spirit of Vanilla. Balance is also a laughing matter. I would rather said spirit is kept despite trying to negate the mess that has been caused, but I think we're getting farther from the point of being able to justify that through means of RP and old-fashioned Vanilla/WC3 essence.

> In BGs, even with OG vanilla classes, every class has a spot just by BGs being more "complex" of a game with roles like warding bases, carrying flags etc. Also always felt small scale pvp is somehow more boring and more stressful at the same time than BGs like AB.

Depends; personally I live for 1v1s, WPvP in various locations and under diverse circumstances, and especially organic and random XvXs. BGs are more fun for the objectives and the teamplay aspect to remind you there are way more people possibly working together given that this is (supposed to be) an MMORPG.

> Why not use the defensive stats that are already in the original game to lower pvp damage instead? Like adding even more stamina and maybe armor/resistances to pvp gear while trading in lower damage compared to pve stuff? Wouldn't be OP in pve since everybody tries to go for dps/hps/tps anyways I could imagine? Or am I missing something here?

Tanks. Tanks would be on steroids as a result of this. People here including myself are trying to do mental gymnastics on the matter precisely to try to already prepare such changes to specifically cater to PvP without making a mockery of PvE (even though it has already made a mockery of both itself AND of PvP). To have new gear with far-higher STA, Armor, RES, etc., would make it applicable in PvE, as those are pre-existing Stats already utilized in all parts of the game. People with access to such proposed gear, therefore, could leech it off of PvP and make PvE an even bigger cakewalk than it already is, or to circumvent flawed PvE designs of wrongfully implemented difficulty in an equally unintended manner through cheap gear carry from one environment into another just because it so happens to have inflated Stats (much like how custom PvE gear currently has that fact going for itself).

The aim is to do something different. Preferably the good kind of "different".

## Post 124769 by Agentsmith — 2025-03-31T21:22:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124769#p124769 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

There are a lot of good ideas here, but i feel some might take too long to implement or might be a coding nightmare. We need quick fixes.

My opinion:

 1. Yes to rated arenas. Not enough pop for rated bgs. Either add x faction to arena or make it so you can fight against the same faction to improve queue times.

 2. Resilience may sound good on the surface, but it will split the community. Now people complain that pve gear is too strong in pvp, but if resil becomes a really good stat, you will have the opposite issue... Making pvp gear a must to competitively participate.  When implemented in retail, (tbc/wotlk), it acted as a barrier to entry for pvers into pvp. Many didnt wanna grind out another set just to participate in pvp so they opted out of this all together. Especially if honor system is so grindy. But in retail, large population and battlegroups between servers minimized this loss in players. We dont have that. In addition as some have pointed out, it will make certain classes near immortal with resilience (such as paladins) so that will have to be adressed.

 3. If you insist on adding resilience, you have to also make honor system less grindy, (no decay etc) to increase participation. But then the rewards will be less rewarding. Ive seen other servers do this and nearly everyone was r14. This is fine if you make new sets rewarded in rated arena which could also be closer to naxx or kara40 ilvl items to close that gap between pve and pvp gear. On pvp gear: Reduce offensive stats, increase defensive and/or add resil. That way its a trade off and pvp gear doesnt become pve bis. And pve gear can still be somewhat competitive in pvp with more offensive stats but less defensive.

 4. Alternatively, I think instead of adding resilience, you could do one of two things to adress burst:

a) reduce dmg on all player dmg abilities by a good amount (maybe 30%?). Also reduce all monster health in pve by same amount. Basically a number squish across the board (to spells and abilities) without changing the gear/stats. OR

b) implement a debuff in all instanced pvp that reduces all dmg done by a large amount(30%?). Or an hp buff instead and leave the dmg the same (30%?).

Thanks for reading and good luck. Tough to please everyone.

## Post 124782 by Philipred — 2025-03-31T22:14:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124782#p124782 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Hello all,

First and foremost, I’d like to express my appreciation for the incredible work you’ve done in creating a unique and engaging Vanilla+ experience. Your dedication to class balance, new content, and the overall player experience is evident, and I’m sure the community as a whole is grateful for your efforts.

That being said, I would like to bring attention to a significant issue affecting PvP balance: the current state of Paladins. While I understand the design philosophy of making all specs viable in both PvE and PvP, I believe that the current implementation has resulted in unintended consequences that are severely disrupting class balance.

In Vanilla WoW (2004), Paladins were a highly durable class with excellent support capabilities but questionable offensive power. Their strengths included:

Heavy armor (plate) and the ability to use a shield, drastically reducing melee damage taken.

Self-healing and healing others, including instant heals through talents.

The ability to dispel magic, poisons, and diseases on themselves and allies.

Immunity to physical damage through Blessing of Protection (for themselves and others).

The ability to remove all movement impairments (both for themselves and allies).

Stun abilities such as Hammer of Justice.

Complete immunity to all damage and debuffs via Divine Shield, also removing all negative effects.

A powerful emergency heal (Lay on Hands) restoring full health.

A robust suite of buffs for themselves and their team.

Increased movement speed via talents.

The ability to incapacitate targets with Repentance (through talents).

While they were a formidable force in PvP, they were far from overpowered. Their main role was often that of an incredibly resilient healer, and only well-geared Retribution Paladins posed a real offensive threat. However, in Turtle WoW, things have changed dramatically.

Turtle WoW has taken an already durable class and given it immense offensive power. The result? A class that has the highest survivability in the game (even more than Druids, who rely on escape mechanics) while simultaneously being able to hit like a freight train. The impact of this on PvP is undeniable:

Paladins make up approximately 25% of the server’s player base. Given that each faction has access to 9 classes, and Paladins are Alliance-exclusive, this disparity is staggering.

Battlegrounds often feature teams where nearly half (or more) of the opposing players are Paladins.

Duels and world PvP often feel one-sided when facing a Paladin, due to their excessive defensive and offensive capabilities.

If we take a look at all the defensive tools I mentioned earlier, it's clear that fighting a paladin means facing an opponent who can remove debuffs, become immune to slows and physical damage, heal themselves, stun, and even outpace most classes in movement. On top of that, they hit like a truck and wear plate armor, making them incredibly durable.

Sure, some abilities exclude others, you can't pop Lay on Hands right after a bubble, but in any given fight, you'll always have to deal with at least one or more of these defensive mechanics, making them absurdly hard to bring down.

Since the goal is to keep Paladins viable in PvE across all specializations, I propose adjusting their defensive toolkit in PvP without harming their viability elsewhere.

Possible adjustments include:

Make certain defensive abilities exclusive to Holy specialization. For example:

Dispel Magic? Restricted to Holy.

Lay on Hands? Restricted to Holy.

Self-healing for non-Holy Paladins should have a significantly higher mana cost, making it an inefficient option compared to bandaging.

Limit the availability of key survivability abilities based on talent selection.

A Retribution or Protection Paladin should have to sacrifice some defensive abilities in favor of their offensive or tanking toolkit.

Adjust cooldowns or durations on certain abilities in PvP settings.

For example, Divine Shield could have a longer cooldown in PvP or be dispellable by Priest abilities like Mass Dispel (which was introduced later in WoW, but a similar mechanic could be considered).

By implementing changes like these, PvP encounters will feel more balanced without completely gutting the class in PvE. The goal is not to make Paladins unplayable but to bring their overall power level in PvP more in line with other classes.

Why This Matters for the Future of PvP in Turtle WoW? With the upcoming introduction of Arenas, it is crucial to establish a fair and competitive environment. Right now, the overwhelming presence and dominance of Paladins threaten to deter skilled PvP players from engaging in the system altogether. If PvP is meant to be a lasting, engaging part of Turtle WoW, adjustments must be made.

I fully expect that many Paladin players will oppose any changes, after all, nobody likes seeing their class nerfed. However, I firmly believe that addressing these balance issues is necessary for the health of PvP in Turtle WoW. Right now, playing a Paladin in PvP requires minimal effort to achieve outstanding results, which is not a sign of a well-balanced system.

I appreciate the time and consideration given to this topic and hope that the need for balance adjustments is recognized. I’d love to hear thoughts on potential changes and whether class balance adjustments are truly being considered.

Best regards,

## Post 124811 by Vaikaris (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-04-01T02:45:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124811#p124811 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

1) Honor is not an issue, decay is. Decay has always been the problem. The system was imagined as a sort of contest between the players to develop organically, but it didn't work in vanilla itself and now when we know the formula it's pure garbage. But if we go back to vanilla you'll remember people loved the idea of the slow, difficult r14 grind. And r14 was the true achievement, while r13 was a consolation price for people with less time. Instead of reworking honor, why not just remove decay? Then r14 becomes a slow, difficult, tedious grind, but you can do it over the course of a year or two gradually, similar to how you might tackle raiding if you've got less time. Huge sense of achievement, sense of journey, just overall fun. Yeh, then everyone will probably go for it, but you can also just increase the honor from r13 to r14 significantly so that it's almost equal to all the ranks before it.

As for r13 being reachable through a consistent grind - why not? r10 gear is MC tier yet it requires investment of multiple weeks of 40+ hours to reach. The time investment for MC tier gear is equivalent to a hundred BWL runs, somehow. And r13 gear is somewhere around BWL and AQ40 yet it requires the time investment equivalent of dozens of naxx runs. Wouldn't it make sense that r13 gear is easier? And if decay gets removed and you just increase honor from 13 to 14, it will slot itself nicely. It's not going to make anything easier, because in a server of full t3s everywhere, being able to spend months gearing up to be LESS geared than them is not major.

Not to mention you already made arena gear reachable through less grinding which in some instances is similar. So it makes no sense not to remove decay complete. Just an unfun, terrible mechanic that has no reason being in game and prevents fun.

 2) Tough question. Ranked and rated stuff is fun, but we for sure don't have the pop as you say. Plus it'll promote more poopsocking, which we really don't need here. The ONLY way I see a fun ranked system existing is to capitalize on one of the very fun aspects of vanilla that disappeared - duelling. 1v1 arenas where you introduce a spell damage/weapon damage cap equivalent to MC gear (so basically if you have more than like 300-400 spell damage it's capped or your weapon damage is capped at like 74 dps/310 damage for a two-handed). Then add a ranking system similar to wotlk rating where you can't lose rating up to 1500.

Solo shuffle is also fun, but I question solo shuffle in 3v3 here. You won't get the healer + dps + dps balance.

 3) Resilience had three effects - it made pvp gear mandatory for pvp, thus removing the coolness of getting pve loot to flex on others, it made pvp way less bursty and more interesting AND it made pvp WAY more boring. WOTLK resilience was heaps better for the third part and it even meant you could experiment with some pve gear and bring back the coolness a bit. Vanilla pvp has several issues - naxx weapons are way, way, way, way overtuned and when you're at that level you can also freely spam consumables at will. On the other hand, casters also end up at one-shot territory. You won't solve these unless you add proper resilience that can mitigate most of the crit damage, so going down this route would have to be done very seriously. If at all possible though you should really consider nerfing single ability damage in pvp somehow - if shamans can't one-shot you with chain lightning and warriors with might of menethil can't dump out 2k crits at will, you CAN still have fun in pvp. It's really the upper end that needs fixing, not the general situation.

If you do go with resilience just flat out nerfing damage, pvp will be absolutely inaccessible up until you're at least fully MC+ geared. Because for example a warrior with entry level pvp gear of blues and an epic weapon has about 20% crit chance or so. If you have 10% reduction in being crit, that warrior can't touch you and it'd be very boring for him. I don't have a perfect solution here, I just think it's worth pointing out the problem is in the upper end of gear.

## Post 124818 by Crake (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-04-01T04:37:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124818#p124818 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Drubarrymooer wrote: Mon Mar 31, 2025 10:48 am**
> IDK what exactly you mean by "such little investment".  I've invested more gold and hours than I'd care to count to get near bis.  It wasn't a "little investment" my any means.
>
>  Instacast dots are, afaik, quite mana expensive.  Your suggestion effectively doubles the amount of mana spent for the same DMG.  Should melee abilities cost double the amount of rage or energy?  Of course not.  That would be a terrible solution.

I'm talking action economy investment in a pvp engagement, not gold/time investment outside of the engagement. The investment of applying a dot on someone is... a gcd. In exchange, you often get upward of 2k damage on someone. If you're a class with multiple dots, that can stack up quickly.

Mana cost wise, they are more expensive than other spells, yes, but commesurate with their damage per cast, and the fact that they don't have a cast time, meaning they can't be interrupted, and many classes don't have a dispel to deal with them, that means there's no way to play around them. This makes them immensely problematic in pvp, when you can just throw them out while constantly on the move, fire and forget, and then focus on defensive plays while still destroying your opponent's hp.

Try comparing shadow word pain, moonfire, corruption, curse of agony to other instant cast, instant damage abilities. If they did damage comparable to a fireblast in pvp over the course of 6 seconds, rather than the damage of a pyroblast over 18 seconds I think it would be much more healthy for pvp.

The only other solution is giving classes some form of dot removal like dance of shadows, or aspect of the turtle, but I think we can all pretty universally agree that's NOT something we want in turtle wow.

> **Bjorn88 wrote: Mon Mar 31, 2025 11:29 am**
> > **Crake wrote: Mon Mar 31, 2025 9:48 am**
> > Potential solutions:
> >  - Set a hard crit cap against players
> >  - Reduce critical damage vs players (From 200% damage down to 150% or even 125%)
> >  - Have an internal cooldown on critical strikes against players
> >  - a stack -> crit system giving some form of control over when critical strikes occur, at the expense of the frequency of critical strikes. Might step on the toes of some abilities like cold blood that can guarantee a crit already
>
>   Hard disagree. This will screw over burst-dependent classes like warriors and shamans significantly. Tinkering with mechanics in a plan-economy style will not solve the issues.
>
>  Having some version of resilience will be the way forward. Also buff pvp gear stam to be superior to equivalent pve gear, while reducing the stam on the latter. TBC had the stat differences between pvp and pve gear done well.

I'm not sure if you read the rest of my post, but i followed up with the idea that pvp sets would remove the crit reduction. Everyone starts with the resilience already baked in and pvp gear overcomes it. That way pvp gear doesn't provide survivability, it provides pvp damage above and beyond what pve gear would normally provide. That way two people in pve gear doesn't result in a glass cannon match, but someone in pvp gear will annihilate someone in pve gear. Meanwhile two people in pvp gear will have sufficient survivability baked into their gear at the expense of dps stats that they won't result in a glass cannon match off either.

Another option that I once floated on the blizzard forums was allowing players to buy free pvp gear (rank 14 or arena if that comes in), but that free gear is EXCLUSIVE to instanced pvp areas, and provides no stats or abilities outside of them. This allows instanced pvp to be instantly accessible to players at even footings with other players, making it a more competitive environment, and the rewards would be sets of pvp gear that can be used OUTSIDE of instanced pvp, giving pvp players an edge in world pvp as their reward.

## Post 124832 by Wolf of Rage (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-04-01T06:23:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124832#p124832 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Jackbonus wrote: Tue Apr 01, 2025 5:58 am**
> To all the people suggesting instanced buffs, special gear that only works in battlegrounds etc. Please for the love of god no! ![🙏](//cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/twitter/twemoji@latest/assets/svg/1f64f.svg)
>  Let us keep our characters consistent throughout the world. Let me be exactly the same wether I fight someone in BRM, inside WSG or duelling outside SW.
>  It's an abysmal idea and it will destroy the entire identity of your character

With proper implementations and balancing, none of that should destroy your Character's identity in either scene.

If improperly done, or none of these are even put into effect, then the current scene will remain, and balance will keep on being inexistent, which is worse here compared to original Vanilla's own old balancing issues.

The only other way would be to wholly rebalance everything -- from Races, to Classes, to Specializations, to Equipment, to Consumables -- to a degree at which both PvE and PvP will be... somewhat better.

But that would be annoying. It would also definitely not be doable to the degree people want -- some things just cannot be balanced for PvP in such a manner, without messing with PvE, and likewise, the opposite would be true as well.

Some of the suggested ideas are actually from or akin to later versions of WoW where they got implemented precisely to try and enforce some balance -- because as things kept progressing, it simply ceased to be possible to keep applying the same methods of continuing balance. It has gotten to a similar point here in this very server. So, those later ideas sound more appealing, because, otherwise... there isn't much else to be done at this point. Kara40 will launch. New, bigger, better gear will be acquirable. Stats will, once again, be on the up and up. At some point this place might as well tease TBC numbers even further in time from now, without actually officially being TBC.

So I would agree with you and be against all this as well, but you have to try and see it from a broader perspective and realize that it's highly unlikely or even not doable to keep things balanced through simpler and more old-fashioned means true to Vanilla anymore.

> > I'm talking action economy investment in a pvp engagement, not gold/time investment outside of the engagement. The investment of applying a dot on someone is... a gcd. In exchange, you often get upward of 2k damage on someone. If you're a class with multiple dots, that can stack up quickly.
>
>  Yes, 1 gcd for 2k dmg out of 4k+ if not much more if you're gearing and enchanting for pvp.  On my feral druid, I've opened up with 2.4k ravage crits before.  That's 1 gcd.  I'm sure rogues and warriors have other similar bursty moves.  I've been crit with 2k+ holy strikes before.  It looks like very similar action economies to me.

We shouldn't be comparing the rng of crits, especially with abilities that require positioning and set up to actually execute, to a fire and forget, ranged, instant cast ability with a very flat damage curve. Sure you've gotten 2.4k ravage crits and I've hit similar crits on my rogue with ambush, but ravage requires being in stealth, closing distance, and being behind the target, and THEN getting lucky on a crit. Rogues specced for ambush have it a bit easier, since they get 60% extra crit when ambushing, but they still have all of the set up requirements that said dots do not have. Holy strike and mortal strike crits are likewise just that, crits.

We also need to consider the ramifications of having a constant dot on you, it means no stealthing and no bandaging, and it also means that you basically are forced into an all or nothing engagement, else you're just going to die to hp attrition.

> **Drubarrymooer wrote: Tue Apr 01, 2025 5:20 am**
> > **Crake wrote: Tue Apr 01, 2025 4:37 am**
> > Mana cost wise, they are more expensive than other spells, yes, but commesurate with their damage per cast, and the fact that they don't have a cast time, meaning they can't be interrupted, and many classes don't have a dispel to deal with them, that means there's no way to play around them. This makes them immensely problematic in pvp, when you can just throw them out while constantly on the move, fire and forget, and then focus on defensive plays while still destroying your opponent's hp.
>
>  I'm not going to say "potion", though please recognize that is option.  There will be encounters that melee vs caster won't win...and on the range front, you'd be right.  I do believe rogues just got  pretty sick ranged interrupt.  I'm not sure what there would be for warriors to combat a caster at max range.  But like I said, there are just some battle conditions and class match ups that are either high or low chance to win scenarios.  At close range, especially openers, melee is very strong.

As the changes haven't come through yet, I haven't had a chance to play with the ranged interrupt just yet. I am ASSUMING it will be instant cast (and hopefully instant interrupt), but I assumed that about the previous round of deadly throw changes as well, so time will have to tell. In either case though, dots don't have a cast time, so there's still no recourse there.

And yes, while you're right in the sense that there are going to just be some matchups that are in favour of one side or another, a dot class being able to just do a drive by casting of a few dots shouldn't mean almost certain death for any class without self healing.

> **Drubarrymooer wrote: Tue Apr 01, 2025 5:20 am**
> > **Crake wrote: Tue Apr 01, 2025 4:37 am**
> > Try comparing shadow word pain, moonfire, corruption, curse of agony to other instant cast, instant damage abilities. If they did damage comparable to a fireblast in pvp over the course of 6 seconds, rather than the damage of a pyroblast over 18 seconds I think it would be much more healthy for pvp.
>
>  I don't understand.  If I'm reading you correctly, you want more damage over a shorter time?  Or you want the damage spread out over more time?  If the former, I don't see a problem with that.  That's higher DPS after all.  If its the latter, 18s is an awful long time in a pvp engagement.  18s for 2k vs the melee 2k insta crits.  And you want less damage?  I don't mean this offensively, but that sounds like a player problem.

I am indeed advocating for the higher dps (lower overall damage per cast), shorter duration dots. 18s is roughly the duration of dots currently, its usually something like, 6-9 ticks at 3s intervals, give or take depending on the exact dot. I'm saying up the dps of the dots a bit to compensate for the loss of damage, and reduce it to 6s, or 2-3 ticks, meaning it takes more gcd investment to keep the dots up, but they apply a bit more pressure to compensate, roughly on par with that of using active abilities. Ideally I also think their range should also be reduced to match that of other instant cast abilities, but i don't know if that's necessarily hitting them a bit too hard.

> **Drubarrymooer wrote: Tue Apr 01, 2025 5:20 am**
> > **Crake wrote: Tue Apr 01, 2025 4:37 am**
> > The only other solution is giving classes some form of dot removal like dance of shadows, or aspect of the turtle, but I think we can all pretty universally agree that's NOT something we want in turtle wow.
>
>  I think many a vanilla player would agree that lack of class homogeneity is a good thing.  Not all classes should have the same cleansing abilities.  Similarly, not all classes NEED aoe abilities.

Yup, I think we're all on the same page for this one.

> **Jackbonus wrote: Tue Apr 01, 2025 5:58 am**
> To all the people suggesting instanced buffs, special gear that only works in battlegrounds etc. Please for the love of god no! ![🙏](//cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/twitter/twemoji@latest/assets/svg/1f64f.svg)
>  Let us keep our characters consistent throughout the world. Let me be exactly the same wether I fight someone in BRM, inside WSG or duelling outside SW.
>  It's an abysmal idea and it will destroy the entire identity of your character

I want to note that my suggestion of instanced pvp gear isn't to have special pvp gear for instances, it's to have the same pvp gear, but anyone can wear it in instances, and only ranked people can wear it out in the world. Mechanically, it makes pvp more even and competitive, along with having a much lower barrier to entry, and lore wise it makes total sense, factions equip their army when they send them off to war, but soldiers don't get to use the faction's equipment for their own personal endeavours unless they've earned it.

## Post 124857 by Apoplex — 2025-04-01T07:45:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124857#p124857 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Firstly, thank you devs for consistently trying to improve the experience on TWOW even further and also for trying to making it a good experience for everyone.

If all vanilla nostalgics are honest, they have to admit that the PVP system was/is a pure grindfest and at best a small addition to the PVE endgame content. It was only TBC that allowed “pure” PVP players to enjoy the game in their own way. Without an exclusive PVP itemization and incentives to actually improve your skills rather than just put it in the effort and grind, any "pure" PVP becomes meaningless.

That being said, it is still fair to also warn about potential problems of PVP gear, resistance or similar stat philosophies as well as balancing issues between PVP and PVE. The latter has always been Blizzards main issue.

All in all, this is a great announcement for any PVP enthusiast and is a huge step towards the right direction.  turtle_in_love_head   Meaning a holistic WOW experience for every player, not only the PVE community. This will really make your server stand out even more. In contrast to Blizzard, I have complete trust in the turtle wow devs to make this happen, be considerate, learn from your mistakes and correct them, and not falter in the face of criticism from a loud minority of the community. The past proved that you are equipped for that task.

## Post 124911 by Argieman — 2025-04-01T13:40:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=124911#p124911 | page 3 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Tailoring your game arround "Minigames", instead of balancing them with open world, is against vanilla philosophy and not a good idea. It's about the power creep, you want to be strong? then grind.

There are things you can implement on the top of my head to make things faster (queues, gearing, etc.), namely a harsh time cap for bgs, penalty for flag carriers, diminishing returns on long bgs, reduced honor capstones for the blue pvp items, increase the rep gain from pvp factions, create alternative pvp gear according races/factions that gain rep for participaing in all bgs, reduced ress timers for the winning side, etc.

You don't need resilence, you can increase armor on blue gear and resistances on epic gear, this is also good for an alternative gearing path for dungeon/early raid tanks, specially bears.

On the topic of classes balances, cc, roots, etc. Further expansions have solved it by mechanics, like wotlk warriors have a quicker reflect and a self heal when rooted. There's no need to give them let's say more leap or ways to break root, or dispels.

## Post 125015 by Crake (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-04-02T02:00:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125015#p125015 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

The gear diff issue is something that needs to be addressed, right now the power difference between a fresh 60 and a nax geared character is on the level of a level 50 vs a level 60, and that was solved by having the two characters be in separate brackets.

The issue with having gear brackets though is it will further segment the already small pvp playerbase.

The only other solution is to minimize the gear diff. On the one hand we can nerf gear in pvp, but I feel that would be universally disliked. That leaves the only other solution being raising the gear floor, which brings me back to pvp gear that is universally available, but for instances only.

## Post 125031 by Qz5733 — 2025-04-02T05:19:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125031#p125031 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Hello, I have gone through two R14 processes here and I have some thoughts,
The R10 R13 set has the effect of reducing all damage by 5% for each item, and the overall effect should reach about 30%, because the damage now exceeds the original T3 damage of level 60 through the 172 talent modification and everyone's equipment getting better and better. I think this is necessary. As for the profession modification and equipment attribute modification, I don't think everyone can like it.
For example, in 1V1, I am a warrior who cannot defeat a paladin, but I have a little bit of AOE that he does not have. You will not weaken the damage of a paladin. Even a level 51 paladin can deal half of my health. I am a T2.5 warrior.
In addition, I think modifying the Blood Ring Battlefield and Sunshine Forest Battlefield before joining the rating battlefield or arena may be a very good way to increase reputation. Nowadays, no one is playing these two battlefields at all, and they are all made by you. Sunshine Forest's Time Reputation is too little, and the Blood Ring Reputation has been almost weakened by you. Sunshine Forest needs to improve the way it ends the game
Another issue is armor. In current PVP, only Thief Hunter Warriors face the problem of high armor and reduced damage, while all other professions can ignore this problem. For example, Druids have spell damage, Priests have spell damage, Mages and Warlocks have spell damage, Paladins have spell damage, Shamans also have spell damage, and Hunters' survival talents can ignore armor reduction because they rely on flames. Only Warriors and Thieves will be affected by armor reduction. These two professions are very weak in PVP now, with slow damage instead of spell 3K4K bursts.
The above is all about PVP, which has nothing to do with PVE. I understand that you adjust your profession based on PVE, but you have completely changed PVP

>   With this, we would much prefer to change the balance of PvP via itemization, rather than bloating abilities with pvp-exclusive conditionals.

It doesn't have to be very sophisticated.
My proposal regarding pvp progression is: initial gear provide you with lots of hp. The more skilled you get, the more damage you deal.

For example:
kara40 item has 40 strength and 30 stamina
top tier pvp item has 40 stamina and 30 strength
initial pvp item available to fresh 60lvl has 60 stamina and 10 strength

This way, new players will be beefy and could survive bursts. The more they "prove" their skill in rated arenas, the more damage they can deal. Though, more damage mean less survivability.

## Post 125205 by Zvyrhol (Grandmaster of Forum PvP) — 2025-04-03T09:55:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125205#p125205 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I haven't read whole discussion so I don't know if it has already been proposed by someone. Here is my suggestion to change Honor system.

Remove Honor decay completely. Grinding should be longer but the system shouldn't punish for skipping PvP in some weeks.

From my perspective current Honor system is generally OK but has one con. It's benefitial mainly to players who play PvP week by week and don't have a break. It's punishing for players who play from time to time but due to life reasons they can't affort to grind as much Honor to break weekly threshold. I know many good and active PvP players on Turtle who haven't reached R14 yet but they play PvP for years. In contrast, there are people who commit 4 months of their life only to grind rank 8 hours a day everyday - and the completely leave PvP once they reach R14 and don't show up in battlegrounds ever again. I think this is the main issue of current system - this system can reward players who quit once they reach R14 but the same system can't reward active players who sometimes must skip some weeks. I am the example - I am stuck on R11 because I often take long breaks from game so Honor decay kills my progression to R12. If there was no decay, I would be able to hit R14 eventually (in long future but still). Grinding 500k in one week is no problem for me, decay is.

The above suggestion I propose would fix this issue really. Everyone will be happy and the fix is easy. To compensate this change (because it's beneficial for everyone) I suggest increasing the time needed to grind (so if you could grind 500k Honor a week and could get R14 in 4 weeks, now you would have to grind 500k a week but for 6 weeks let's say).

Also @Akalix, I think your observation on Honor system is a bit wrong.

> **Akalix wrote: Sun Mar 30, 2025 7:55 pm**
> **Honor Rework**
>
>  The current implementation of **Honor** is a slightly more forgiving version of the original vanilla system, where the optimal way to rank up is not improving your gameplay, it's increasing how much time you spend grinding honor.

Improving your gameplay was never the most important thing in Vanilla PvP in order to acquire better rank. And it shouldn't be. Nothing is wrong with spending time. Rank 14 is long goal and challenge - it shouldn't be acquired based on your "skill". If it was acquired by being good in PvP, casuals would never reach it while PROs would get it in 1 month in theory.

>
>  You wouldn't be able to just enter arena's like you're stating here in the first place.
>
>  Back in tbc you had to make an arena team and then find someone that wanted to join it, no one will join your team if you walk around in crap gear lol. Especially not end gear players.
>
>
>  So you will first need to get gear to team up to begin with.

In order to be able to go past 1500 rating you needed roughly an hour a day, but you could freely skip a day, or even an entire week and you'd make up most of it. Daily BG quest was also wonderful, even more so in WOTLK, because it gave a choice.

As it is right now, to be able to TOUCH t3 geared people you'll need r13 gear, which is roughly a 50 hour per week investment for roughly 15 weeks straight. As in, 750 hours, where you only get to take a break maybe once or twice and only at the start (since at r12 even if you can't derank, losing progress into the rank is brutal and requires a whole week or so to make up). This is the ENTRY level.

It's also utterly pointless, since its the equivalent of BWL gear, which requires ~3 hours per week for about 4-5 weeks. Give or take. R10 gear requires at least 30-40 hours per week for lets say, 4 weeks. It's the equivalent of t0.5, which can be done in about a week.

And here's the kicker - IF you dedicate your life to farming this and take no breaks and grind like a maniac, you'll still get utterly smashed by a t3 caster who can one tap you.

And the further kicker - this will get WORSE because better PvE gear is coming.

Now, you might say, not everyone has t3 gear etc. and that's totally true. But if I enter a BG or arena and there's a chance a person will deny me the ability to play completely and I can basically just afk, I just won't do it. Why would I?

It's an absurd, absurd difference between pve and pvp gear. Just the simple fact pvp gear requires you to take no breaks - EVEN without rank decay you still have to grind it out in one go because past r10 progress slows to a crawl and you need several weeks in a row to rank up. That's literally a separate level of commitment to raiding. And even ignoring this we're talking a 50+ times bigger investment for WORSE gear.

Thsi never happened in TBC.

## Post 125424 by Skattberg — 2025-04-04T10:45:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125424#p125424 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Akalix wrote: Sun Mar 30, 2025 7:55 pm**
> **Greetings!**
>
>  Over time as we have gone further down the path of **Vanilla+** and departed from 1:1 vanilla parity, we've made significant changes to all aspects of the game. With this in mind, there are a handful of potential new features and design choices we would like feedback on before implementing. Please note that we reserve the right to make unpopular decisions if we think they will be beneficial to the overall health of the network. As of now, we are not confirming any of these changes, only confirming that we plan to do **something**.
>
>
>  **Honor Rework**
>
>  The current implementation of **Honor** is a slightly more forgiving version of the original vanilla system, where the optimal way to rank up is not improving your gameplay, it's increasing how much time you spend grinding honor. The system, especially at the higher levels, requires an unsustainable amount of queue spamming. There are numerous ways we could change this system—and we would like to know what changes the community would like to see, including if people would rather see no changes at all.
>
>
>  **Rated PvP**
>
>  **Rated PvP** is a commonly requested feature, as the current Honor system doesn't serve as a true skill indicator, rather a indication of time played. At our current population, we **do not** plan to add a rated Battleground feature, as we do not have the PvP population to support it. However, we are considering adding **Rated Arena Teams** and/or a **Solo Shuffle**-esq queue. These appeal to two different types of players, with arena teams being premade and coordinating their play, and a solo shuffle queue perfect for players who would rather log on and "just go" rather than wait for a team.
>
>
>  **PvP Itemization Update**
>
>  Currently because there's limited ways to itemize specifically for PvP, PvP encounters are very bursty, it's kill or be killed.
>
>  This was "solved" after vanilla by introducing full item sets with Resilience on them.
>
>  We would like the community's input on ways we could limit this burst-paced gameplay and have already written out a small list of things we think might work:
> - **PvP Set Bonuses** which would give strong effects for PvP combat.
>
> - **PvP (instanced) Item Buffs** akin to retail's current approach, which buffs PvP items significantly when in PvP.
>
> - **Resilience** or a similar system, which provides stats specifically beneficial to PvP from PvP gear.
>   With this, we would much prefer to change the balance of PvP via itemization, rather than bloating abilities with pvp-exclusive conditionals.
>
>
>  **Closing**
>
>  In closing, I would like to reiterate that none of these changes are directly confirmed—only a handful of concepts that we are open to pursuing if our community is interested in them. Please let us know your feedback on these ideas, as we are actively looking for our community's opinions.

Yes! Finally some love to pvp! Here are my thoughts:

Honor system
This, along with gear, is for me the most pressing issue. The no rank decay change imo was a good choice, being FORCED to play quickly makes it a chore which makes it no longer fun. I'd like to see an expansion of this system with no decay at all, but with a harder ladder to climb, and maybe a decay for the final rank to make making it to the top worth it and something unique, as it is now. But also more gear along the way as rewards for climbing, and maybe even more ranks.

Rated PvP
Imo, in retail wow i never cared about ranks. What made pvp fun was making friends and enemies, recognizing people on your side to assist and hunting good players on the enemy side. I dont think forcing new play styles to decide whos good at pvp and whos not is a healthy way. That shows in the bgs by stats and recognition by name. Personally i think a wide and including pvp environment makes for the best gameplay.

PvP itemization
I'd like to see small steps in this area. Imo resilience in retail separated the PvP and the PvE scenes too much. A new stat like something on the Bloodring gear would be a good start. Like: "Equip: reduces damage taken from players by 3% and increases damage to players by 1%" and have the stat on a few slot items so that it gives an edge over geared players but not too much to take away the fun in big damages.
But a larger variety of gear is very much needed as pvp rewards.

## Post 125462 by RPGnerd (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-04-04T14:53:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125462#p125462 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

The ranking system only benefits in-game mafias.

The proper system should be based on the number of Honor Kills.

For example, Rank 14 could be achieved with 100,000 Honor Kills.

To address a potential issue where players might log multiple accounts to farm HKs, once a player from the opposite faction is killed in the open world, they would only grant Honor Kills again after 10 minutes.

Players who have just been killed receive a debuff called "Recently Slain" that lasts 10 minutes, and could display the name of the player who killed them. They won’t grant Honor Kills again until the debuff expires.

This limitation would not apply inside Battlegrounds.

*Last edited by RPGnerd on Fri Apr 04, 2025 2:57 pm, edited 1 time in total.*

## Post 125533 by SAMYAZA78 — 2025-04-05T10:40:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125533#p125533 | page 4 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Sorry about the wall of text, Inever post on forums but I'm passionate about PVP.

Do NOT change the Honor system. Seriously, don’t touch it. Hundreds of players have spent countless hours grinding through it, and changing it now would be a slap in the face to all that effort. The only fix it ever needed is already in place: Honor no longer decays below your current rank. That’s enough.

Do NOT introduce any rating-based system. I’ve played Arenas since the day they were introduced, and they’ve always been a breeding ground for toxicity. It’s nothing but meta comps dominating, in case of turtle wow it would be like double Paladins nuking everyone, while skilled but non-meta players are left in the dust.
Just look at ranked games in LoL or Dota: it’s a cesspool of bitter and angry people. Don’t bring that garbage here.

The real issue is power creep. That’s the root of the problem, not Honor, not ratings. Casters are critting for 3k like it’s nothing. DoTs are ticking for 400+ damage. That’s what needs to be addressed. Not some overcomplicated rating system.

And about resilience, please don’t add TBC or WotLK-style resilience or PvP-only scaling. That kind of lazy design only hurts the game. All it does is split the community: new players or those without PvP gear get obliterated and never come back.
PvE gear should still be usable in PvP, just not optimal. The current 3% crit damage reduction is fine, and even that could be avoided if power creep is properly managed. No one should be eating 3k crits from 40 yards away with no cooldowns.

The only thing that truly needs to be maintained is this:
Rank 14 PvP gear must remain best-in-slot for PvP. The Honor grind is by far the most effort-intensive system in the game. The reward should reflect that.
Right now, PvP gear is already being outclassed by AQ gear. Naxx is next, then Kara 40. If you don’t keep scaling PvP gear to match raid content, the entire system falls apart.

 1 small thing to add about pvp fear Blizzard already had right idea with rank 14 gear, stats were leaning into extra stamina/armor and you could feel in the slow paced pvp of vanilla that it worked you could feel when you are fighting someone in r14 or r10 gear they were always beefer and harder to take down.
Currently in turtle that doesn't work anymore, i can see people in t3 over 6k hp getting 2 shotted by 3k hp mages or any other caster for that matter.
But maybe it can be similar style, maybe instead of resilience, introduce spell damage reduction, there is no spell damage reduction in the game except resistances which no one stacks, no pvp or decent pve items have any resistances.

Think about it if you are getting better gear, extra armor, dodge, parry reduces physical damage, and its always limited to melee range, but you can have the heaviest armor in the game and still do 0% reduction to spells and get blasted from 40 yards away without any chance to do anything about it.
