## Post 125577 by Whalemilk (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-04-05T17:49:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125577#p125577 | page 5 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Akalix wrote: Sun Mar 30, 2025 7:55 pm**
> **Greetings!**
>
>  Over time as we have gone further down the path of **Vanilla+** and departed from 1:1 vanilla parity, we've made significant changes to all aspects of the game. With this in mind, there are a handful of potential new features and design choices we would like feedback on before implementing. Please note that we reserve the right to make unpopular decisions if we think they will be beneficial to the overall health of the network. As of now, we are not confirming any of these changes, only confirming that we plan to do **something**.
>
>
>  **Honor Rework**
>
>  The current implementation of **Honor** is a slightly more forgiving version of the original vanilla system, where the optimal way to rank up is not improving your gameplay, it's increasing how much time you spend grinding honor. The system, especially at the higher levels, requires an unsustainable amount of queue spamming. There are numerous ways we could change this system—and we would like to know what changes the community would like to see, including if people would rather see no changes at all.
>
>
>  **Rated PvP**
>
>  **Rated PvP** is a commonly requested feature, as the current Honor system doesn't serve as a true skill indicator, rather a indication of time played. At our current population, we **do not** plan to add a rated Battleground feature, as we do not have the PvP population to support it. However, we are considering adding **Rated Arena Teams** and/or a **Solo Shuffle**-esq queue. These appeal to two different types of players, with arena teams being premade and coordinating their play, and a solo shuffle queue perfect for players who would rather log on and "just go" rather than wait for a team.
>
>
>  **PvP Itemization Update**
>
>  Currently because there's limited ways to itemize specifically for PvP, PvP encounters are very bursty, it's kill or be killed.
>
>  This was "solved" after vanilla by introducing full item sets with Resilience on them.
>
>  We would like the community's input on ways we could limit this burst-paced gameplay and have already written out a small list of things we think might work:
> - **PvP Set Bonuses** which would give strong effects for PvP combat.
>
> - **PvP (instanced) Item Buffs** akin to retail's current approach, which buffs PvP items significantly when in PvP.
>
> - **Resilience** or a similar system, which provides stats specifically beneficial to PvP from PvP gear.
>   With this, we would much prefer to change the balance of PvP via itemization, rather than bloating abilities with pvp-exclusive conditionals.
>
>
>  **Closing**
>
>  In closing, I would like to reiterate that none of these changes are directly confirmed—only a handful of concepts that we are open to pursuing if our community is interested in them. Please let us know your feedback on these ideas, as we are actively looking for our community's opinions.

Now... I don't PVP, but.

I have two ideas. Have a set of very strong PVP gear that can only be equipped inside a BG. maybe some kind of chest that keeps your PVP stuff in it and at the end of a match it auto-unequips your gear and puts it into the chest. To obtain best gear, you must reach exalted reputation with each BG faction. No more honor system, just basic reputation.

which leads me to my next idea.

It would be cool to have sub-reputations for each BG faction. So for example, Silverwing Sentinels have a level 10-19,  20-29, 30-39, etc. You would be able to reach exalted reputation for each faction which would reward the best pvp gear for that bracket. So for the twinks, they can get exalted and get the best gear.

## Post 125588 by Vaikaris (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-04-05T20:10:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125588#p125588 | page 5 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Wolf of Rage wrote: Sat Apr 05, 2025 8:03 pm**
> You do not know what Retail is, then. Or you're fishing with weak bait.
>
>  But, no. No I'm not.

Well we've already given enough excuse for devs not to fix pvp so might as well carry on.

Retail created the formulaic gear that is based on tiers and you acquire the appropriate tier to perform at the appropriate level. This was introduced in WOTLK with the prefix-gladiator gear as you described that corresponded with a tier set and both were THE appropriate gear to use in the appropriate patch. This patch-based gameplay is what differentiates retail with vanilla. Yes, WOTLK and TBC are a special bit because they're the TRANSITION from vanilla to retail, but these trends that they started define retail.

Formulaic gear is a critical part of it and it's something twow is sadly also introducing that's departing from vanilla. In vanilla you made your own gear set and sets were just a fun thing you COULD do if you wanted - the gear you'd usually go for was a mix of pieces from here and there, with one special thing about vanilla being it's fun to kind of craft your own gear set if you're a hybrid where the set pieces don't work for you. Or go back to MC to get gear that's useful for naxx - lots of these.

Patch-based gameplay where you simply fill a checklist and fit the requirements to fill in the next checklist is the pure spirit of retail.

## Post 125633 by Rhope79 — 2025-04-06T04:39:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125633#p125633 | page 5 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Crazy burst damage should only be a thing when someone takes time to line something up and their entire spec is based around it and its on a CD.  Or should be a thing when someone is multiple tiers of gear behind someone else.

The problem with PvP is that it becomes exacerbatedly imbalanced since its release in 2004.

The reason for this is the devs want us players to be excited to engage in the game and people want to pew pew.

So in the past the original item gear template that in a way was balanced throwing the kitchen sink at the wall in making every geartype even through having every type of green stat based item and having the T1 pieces be just an almagamation of having a large amount of all stats.

However ever since that initial design almost all patches tend towards gear that stacks almost all damage stats.

Even warriors tanks just use stoneshield potion and dual wield for crazy fury damage. (That is busted what happened to tanks)

The result is that the only universal pvp defensive stat is stamina, with armor being important against rogue/war and to a lesser extend pally/hunt.

So you have gear with stat points for Strength, agility, attack power, hit, haste, and because of the tax on having an item have a single stat stamina can never catch up by itself.

And alot of the time those items have a mediocrumb of stamina anyway as they are designed to put most of the stats point potential of the items into dps.

This is because people fancy themselves more as one who can knock down a tower than be a tower as playing defensively is seen as boring.

Also unless you play paladin then every other class is built around having a dps rotation, not a defense rotation.

So you have most of the items are stacked hard for damage potential, and all classes have decent dps rotations and the result is everyone is glass.

It also doesnt help that the max PvP gear (which is meant to be more survival based but ends up being more 60-40 in damage favor) is two tiers behind the current content.

 --
Also I would say about the top rank prestige is something that should be kept but decay should be eliminated and the cap of 500k honor a week being implemented is healthy for the game.

Its still 8-10 weeks to get r14 which takes commitment but isnt unhealthy for ones life to get.

Also I'd recommend a new r15 rank for entirely new sets of Kharazan ilvl gear that is serious about having high amounts of stamina as options for gearing.  Set bonuses are cool but they should be minimal as giving players more options in gearing is a significant portion of customization potential.

Im opposed to introducing resilience as it breaks immersion from PvP to PvE,

like theoretically we are all equal characters and npcs its just characters are those who decided to better themselves rather than sit complacently at a certain powerlevel.  So it doesnt make sense that there would be a stat that differentiates between the two as there is almost no difference from a lore perspective.

Also from a mechanical perspective resilience snowballs hard and its easy to end up close to the bottom of the slippery slope where we have last patch Wotlk resilience problems where players are unkillable because none of the healing is reduced proportionally to the damage reduction.  Higher stamina stats solves this instead.

And if we reduce all healing in PvP then what mechanically immersed lore reason are we giving for that existing?

Also I would recommend many more PvP item options for the lower ranks, having access to just a few underpowered items per rank is devastating.

 --
And Faction leaders dropping PvP gear sounds good on paper but then we're all getting DKs in the city raid and PvP players gearing have to chose between city raids and ranking and if we remove DKs then were banning players left and right for being griefers on quest givers.

The Faction leader idea needs more thought is all Im saying.

Or go back to MC to get gear that's useful for naxx - lots of these.
>
>  Patch-based gameplay where you simply fill a checklist and fit the requirements to fill in the next checklist is the pure spirit of retail.

Completely agree.   Patch based gameplay that only focuses on one area of the game and has powercreep always shrinks the relevant game to that area which shrinks the world which is the main character.

Patches should put equally powerful things across the world that must require a serious effort to attain.  This deepens the world and adds to its richness rather than bleakening it by making it eventually obscure in irrelevancy as the other gear in the small new area is always better.

Having the gear be part of large sets and formulaic also undermines the key attraction of this kind of game which is the choice.

As you will end up with ultimately alot of people using the same pieces and sets as individuality is watered down.

This isnt even taking into account how usually this happens with the min-max crowd.

Its just this is happening because this amount of new gear is small in comparison to the rest available at the level and is stronger and just becomes the default for many specs.

What makes this worse, as Ive said is this all comes most exclusively from one area which shrinks the relevant world and makes the game smaller and less creative

## Post 125640 by Wolf of Rage (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-04-06T06:52:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=125640#p125640 | page 5 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

It is wild to equate what started things as an implementation back some seventeen to eighteen years ago to Retail automatically, especially after having said that immediately after in Cataclysm it was already ruined and became quite dislikeable. That is some basketball-level leaping of logic on display.

The current situation is that there are a slew of problems, and gear is one of them.

Say you revert CC2 back to CC1.

Or say you mass-correct as much as possible in CC2 to bring some order of balance back so that things aren't at least more imbalanced even by Vanilla standards.

Say whatever you want, propose what you will; gear will remain an issue for the sheer fact that currently the PvE side benefits greatly from what it can give, compared to PvP, where even R13 Armor and R14 Weapons are not up to snuff. This needs to be evened out. Doing something like TBC or WotLK at MAX and NOT Cataclysm-like or beyond, will help this. And no, the recent "buff" to said PvP gear is NOT enough... because what are some low-value single-digit increases to Primary Stats and Weapon DpS - - they are so little that they barely make a difference, with your average Joe probably assuming nothing was even done and that such miniscule changes don't even exist, the moment they face someone with alternate T3 - - and soon enough, Kara40 equivalents will also be seen in the PvP scene more commonly.

I feel like some of the responding arguments are being made by PvE mains who want to make sure their farmed-for gear doesn't become less overpowered in the face of PvP equivalents. Or it's like purists with their goggles stuck glued to them while failing to see the irony. That's honestly what this feels like sometimes. This, by itself, is beyond Vanilla, by logic, because even by gear alone, there is a discrepant imbalance that is worse than that originally.

Like even by the end of WotLK you had for instance Warriors with 40-60% PvE gear, depending, meaning both Environments were important to them for their Equipment, unlike in Cata where it got changed to almost 100% with mainly a Trinket and Weapons being PvE only.

> **Crake wrote: Thu Apr 03, 2025 12:06 am**
> > **Xudo wrote: Wed Apr 02, 2025 4:41 pm**
> > It doesn't have to be very sophisticated.
> >  My proposal regarding pvp progression is: initial gear provide you with lots of hp. The more skilled you get, the more damage you deal.
>
>   Rewarding the better people with better gear does nothing but widen the gap and create an ivory tower situation, where new pvpers stand no chance against the entrenched population, and therefore have no opportunity to develop their skills, leading to them disengaging from pvp entirely.
>
>  I do agree however, that there needs to be more options regarding pvp gear and how people can mix and match it, however it shouldnt be a high vs low skill thing, and certainly the higher dps gear shouldnt be locked behind rating or rank.

You right. If game will reward people for "skill" then average people won't have intencive to play at all. First they will be dominated by more skilled player. After some time, average players will be dominated by more skilled players with better gear.

Modern pvp games with persistent progress are all grind-based. Important thing is that they reward players even if they participated in encounter but lost it.
But reward purely for loss is bad idea too. It is present in battlegrounds and it will lead to afk-battles.
Any gear progression system should encourage completing (or at least trying to complete) objectives.

## Post 126012 by Snipesta (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-04-08T16:13:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=126012#p126012 | page 5 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Whalemilk wrote: Sat Apr 05, 2025 5:49 pm**
> > **Akalix wrote: Sun Mar 30, 2025 7:55 pm**
> > **Greetings!**
> >
> >  Over time as we have gone further down the path of **Vanilla+** and departed from 1:1 vanilla parity, we've made significant changes to all aspects of the game. With this in mind, there are a handful of potential new features and design choices we would like feedback on before implementing. Please note that we reserve the right to make unpopular decisions if we think they will be beneficial to the overall health of the network. As of now, we are not confirming any of these changes, only confirming that we plan to do **something**.
> >
> >
> >  **Honor Rework**
> >
> >  The current implementation of **Honor** is a slightly more forgiving version of the original vanilla system, where the optimal way to rank up is not improving your gameplay, it's increasing how much time you spend grinding honor. The system, especially at the higher levels, requires an unsustainable amount of queue spamming. There are numerous ways we could change this system—and we would like to know what changes the community would like to see, including if people would rather see no changes at all.
> >
> >
> >  **Rated PvP**
> >
> >  **Rated PvP** is a commonly requested feature, as the current Honor system doesn't serve as a true skill indicator, rather a indication of time played. At our current population, we **do not** plan to add a rated Battleground feature, as we do not have the PvP population to support it. However, we are considering adding **Rated Arena Teams** and/or a **Solo Shuffle**-esq queue. These appeal to two different types of players, with arena teams being premade and coordinating their play, and a solo shuffle queue perfect for players who would rather log on and "just go" rather than wait for a team.
> >
> >
> >  **PvP Itemization Update**
> >
> >  Currently because there's limited ways to itemize specifically for PvP, PvP encounters are very bursty, it's kill or be killed.
> >
> >  This was "solved" after vanilla by introducing full item sets with Resilience on them.
> >
> >  We would like the community's input on ways we could limit this burst-paced gameplay and have already written out a small list of things we think might work:
> > - **PvP Set Bonuses** which would give strong effects for PvP combat.
> >
> > - **PvP (instanced) Item Buffs** akin to retail's current approach, which buffs PvP items significantly when in PvP.
> >
> > - **Resilience** or a similar system, which provides stats specifically beneficial to PvP from PvP gear.
> >   With this, we would much prefer to change the balance of PvP via itemization, rather than bloating abilities with pvp-exclusive conditionals.
> >
> >
> >  **Closing**
> >
> >  In closing, I would like to reiterate that none of these changes are directly confirmed—only a handful of concepts that we are open to pursuing if our community is interested in them. Please let us know your feedback on these ideas, as we are actively looking for our community's opinions.
>
>   Now... I don't PVP, but.
>
>  I have two ideas. Have a set of very strong PVP gear that can only be equipped inside a BG. maybe some kind of chest that keeps your PVP stuff in it and at the end of a match it auto-unequips your gear and puts it into the chest. To obtain best gear, you must reach exalted reputation with each BG faction. No more honor system, just basic reputation.
>
>  which leads me to my next idea.
>
>  It would be cool to have sub-reputations for each BG faction. So for example, Silverwing Sentinels have a level 10-19,  20-29, 30-39, etc. You would be able to reach exalted reputation for each faction which would reward the best pvp gear for that bracket. So for the twinks, they can get exalted and get the best gear.

I know it was just an idea of yours but I would love to use that "set of strong pvp gear" also in WPVP not constricted to just one thing...

## Post 126651 by OldM00n — 2025-04-13T20:51:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=126651#p126651 | page 6 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Most people agree that PvP is at its best when it’s based on skill, strategy, and creativity. I main a 39 balance druid twink, and it is pretty deflating to be one shot by a shaman in every encounter, or I have to play 1000x harder than they do in order to maybe come out on top. Same is true with rogues, and to some extent, locks.

I can appreciate how difficult this is to balance for PvP outside of PvE. The class changes you’ve made have been fascinating to play around with. I seriously appreciate what you’re doing as a Turtle Team. Despite the dysregulated players channeling the worst of internet rage culture and bashing this project, I feel the majority of us appreciate all your work.

I’ve always believed that gear should be less of a factor in player encounters. The idea could be that all players in BGs have a baseline amount of stats that are more balanced in relation to each other. Or, perhaps big damaging skills can only, at max, take out 25% of a player’s total stamina, and everyone has a baseline of hit points in battlegrounds. I agree with other players that to get those big hit numbers, it should require a set up, not one button. It destroys the game.

Certain classes are obviously rising to the top right now, being played in ways not intended. I know twinks are a little sub-community in the game, but I hope whatever the solution is benefits balanced pvp at lower levels as well, and not just by tinkering with resilience in pvp gear or something.

Thanks for reading.

Your opinion _is_ wrong. Between blue (Rare) and purple (Epic), there can still be huge differences, and not to mention potential Set Bonuses, and more. There are entire worlds of differences just comparing pre-BiS to current BiS in this server, for example.

The numbers will continue becoming a problem as they have been, should they keep rising. But such a suggestion would just make everything pre-BiS and even pre-60 just a headache (it already is for leveling for some Classes like hello Execute and Hamstring changes for Warrior catering to endgame PvE bias).

Your opinion on why it's bad is right. Your idea is also right. But the idea on the _how_ is wrong. So is the opinion on the _why_.

You kinda missed a point. Sure BG introductions changed World PvP appeal but it was still happening at large. You could still random encounter players and had to stood your ground if attacked. BGs are ok. Also i don't mind Arenas however best gear shouldn't be exclusive to best arena rating imho.

About other things
 -Sure you have 30 sec flying mount in Goldshire and you can fly 30 sec around Elywn Forest but its not like TBC mounts where you fly up from anywhere in Outland and you are safe. World lost its original charm and sense of danger with flying mount. You could just fly up from anywhere and be safe from everything. Sure it was only in Outland but everything outside of Outland was useless in TBC for lvl 70 anyway and no real point of going anywhere which was not the case in vanilla and which was another TBC problem. I really don't know and need to put more thought into it to put smart suggestion. I just feel different sets is really bad idea. You should be able to get good loot from Raid or from PvP and from it be stronger and feel safer everywhere like when walking around in dangerous world and when entering BG or when entering dungeon and raids. Having mastered all raids but then get destroyed easily by someone with basic PvP bg's gear was really bad design. And opposite also apply, having mastered arena rating with one of best players on server and then enter basic raid and do pathetic dps with its gear. It feels very very wrong imho.

Please remove any form of progression and power creep. PvP is not PvE, there isn't any stronger bosses to defeat, only virtually stronger players made through gear tiers. Everyone will be on even grounds to fight and more peoples can actually PvP fairly, even if only on occasion.

Some players think that they deserves to one-shot peoples after grinding 2000 hours, and they will legitimate themselves by telling you that you can also suffer being one-shotted for 2000 hours… by them of course. It's void of the thrill of battle.

Killing power creep in PvP won't reduce the fame and the glory that is to be had on the battlegfields. It will merely kills the fancy displays of it.

Absolutely not -- on both accounts.

Not making gear exclusive to best Arena Rating would be repeating the Blood Ring mistakes but with a different flavor to it -- why the hell would people do it then. Or better yet, everyone would do it because it's just more dopamine-fried nonsense of easy and quick rewards so you can go back to AFK-ing in Stormwind or Orgrimmar.
No.

> About other things
>  -Sure you have 30 sec flying mount in Goldshire and you can fly 30 sec around Elywn Forest but its not like TBC mounts where you fly up from anywhere in Outland and you are safe. World lost its original charm and sense of danger with flying mount. You could just fly up from anywhere and be safe from everything. Sure it was only in Outland but everything outside of Outland was useless in TBC for lvl 70 anyway and no real point of going anywhere which was not the case in vanilla and which was another TBC problem. You would just fly around Outland and joing BGs, Arenas, Raids from cities and get summoned and teleported. Many people didn't even leave cities. World lost its appeal and charm and sense of danger and excitement. At least at max level. But that's kinda offtopic so lets return to PvP sets.

 30s at Goldshire/Razor Hill.
 45s at Guild Houses.
You can chain GS into the GH after Northshire and then fly elsewhere from there (like BRM); don't know if RH has such an equivalent. You can also chain GH > GH -- f.ex. Ashenvale GH (Emerald Dream) > Azshara GH (forgot the name), and then, even more hilariously, you can straight-up divebomb into Orgrimmar (yes, as Alliance). And back to the Ash GH, you can fly up to Hyjal/WS with enough +Swim Speed (even the Potion works if you pre-use).
You underestimate this feature's effects because you do not know as much of it nor have you gone to such boundary-breaking extents to see just how much you are capable of doing with it.

> Maybe lower all dmg to players by 50% or something

No.
> Having mastered all raids but then get destroyed easily by someone with basic PvP bg's gear was really bad design.

It's fair. One set of gear is strictly for PvE. It can still be good in PvP. You are just a glass cannon. This was especially evident from WotLK onward, because by the Ruby Sanctum, you had crazy PvE gear with which you could for example be a Hunter and snipe people like a long-range gattling gun. But someone sneezes on you? You're dead.
> And opposite also apply, having mastered arena rating with one of best players on server and then enter basic raid and do pathetic dps with its gear.

Also fair. Gear is PvP-centric. Awarded for PvP participation. Made for PvP for and against Players. Still useful in PvE, but nowhere near the top. Can be used to climb in PvE... not that it's necessary with how easier and quicker it is to speedrun and get carried in PvE, compared to PvP. To this effect, again, still useful up to end-WotLK WoW, because Resilience even had minor Tank benefits (acted in place of/together with Defense for Crit Immunity cap). It would often be shunned, though, due to Resilience being the main big Stat (if a Tank), otherwise Crit/Haste/maybe Mp5 being that (if DD/Healer), which oftentimes resulted in Resilience being a central waste of Stat in PvE. But not fully. It's mostly elitists overexaggerating that. HOWEVER, since Cataclysm and onward? Yeah. Too much. Bad. Nope. Definitely not.
> It feels very very wrong imho.

The way things here are are about as if not more wrong than all that, because PvP is just a dumpster fire with dung used as the burning fuel. And yet people here want to suggest Cross-Faction and Cross-Realm here first before anything... come on...

> **Catpaws wrote: Tue Apr 15, 2025 9:05 am**
> PvP is more fun when fighting similarly geared players.
>
>  Please remove any form of progression and power creep. PvP is not PvE, there isn't any stronger bosses to defeat, only virtually stronger players made through gear tiers. Whatever PvP gears you do, make it free and available to everyone and you'll bolster the battlegrounds population for the better. Everyone will be on even grounds to fight and more peoples can actually PvP fairly, even if only on occasion.
>
>  Some players think that they deserves to one-shot peoples after grinding 2000 hours, and they will legitimate themselves by telling you that you can also suffer being one-shotted for 2000 hours… by them of course. It's void of the thrill of battle.
>
>  Killing power creep in PvP won't reduce the fame and the glory that is to be had on the battlegfields. It will merely kills the fancy displays of it.

You'd just be removing a big part of what makes an RPG... an RPG -- in gameplay terms, anyway: that being grind, gear, and reward. You'd be killing the power thrill. The eyecandy. The just desserts for exploiting someone's lapse in judgement, someone's misplays, and absolutely decimating them, or the same being done to you, you learning, then preventing that the next time (not here, though -- not with this power creep and Class imbalance nonsense). You'd be killing off progression and just giving people more instant rewards (don't we already have enough of those...?), and for what? I feel like this is a suggestion for a different game or just another genre altogether.

This is not Valorant. Not Apex. Not whatever PvP-focal game with strict PvP-only intents. This isn't even the old long-dead Arena-Tournament server (in fact this is more akin to being _somewhat_ of an opposite to it).

You have neither a PvP mindset nor even an RPG one to be making such a suggestion.

## Post 126927 by Bayanni (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-04-15T20:50:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=126927#p126927 | page 6 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

As pointed out in the OP, a large sore point is the lack of a meaningful system to show skill instead of time spent in bgs.

It might be a bit clunky, but adding "medal" tokens for active participation MVP style could mitigate this issue by using the tokens as currency for top-tier gear parallel to C'Thun/Naxx. Give out medals for Most Killing Blows, Most Healing, Most Objectives, Most Damage Dealt, etc. Anything that requires active participation (not for HKs themselves since you could just afk/stealth near the big fights and get more than the actual people fighting).

This avoids the issue of ranked BGs not being possible due to pop and rewards the people carrying their sides, regardless of if they actually won or lost (maybe you could give out mini versions of the medals for winning the bg similar to Bloodring tokens turning into marks?) as they should be rewarded too imo. This could also encourage people to stay in longer and keep going as they'd have a greater chance at medals.

Oh, also, PvP set bonuses would be very much ideal, but I would implement them in a similar fashion to T3 and T3.5 where there are more items in the set than are needed for all the bonuses so there's some room for customization. I'd even go farther and have 7-8 pieces for the sets but have bonuses cap at 5.

That's not the core element of MMORPG, don't know where you got that from. Between level 1 starter and max level BIS can be a huge diference but all level 60s can participate in the same content so doesn't make much sense having that huge diference.

Diferent gear rarities are ok to have diferent power but the bigger issue here is the item level powercreep. A 45 lvl 2h weapon has ~45 dps so the diference in powercreep in lvl 60 is bigger than the diference from level 45 gear to level 60 gear which is ridiculous.
That powercreep also limits a lot itemization, making gearing more about getting whatever piece you can get from the last tier rather than having the freedom to chose an item for its stats/effects without losing way too many dps.
Also the verticality of raids sucks, if you play something of a previous tier or a dungeon anything you get will be much worse than what you already have, so the game becomes focused on playing one raid over and over until you got enough gear for the next one.

## Post 127149 by Siquexy — 2025-04-17T13:30:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=127149#p127149 | page 7 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I was thinking a lot about this topic for past few days. And as I sayed, my experiences with mechanics and things are inferior to those which comment here a lot. But, I noticed through out the years shift in perception of PvP in a game. Bare with me, I do not want to ridicule any playstyle with my thought, or take away anyones fun.

I try to PvP from time to time, mostly to learn it and at least become decent in it. Because you see, when you play WoW and do through your leveling only PvE, you are not much used to PvP. You are not comfortable with it, it easily upsets you and you feel heart pounding in your chest (It's adrenaline feeling not going to lie). But in the end, you queue only once per week or month. And you get back same experience all the time, you get smashed in BG, you manage almost nothing because you lack skill, gear but most importantly **you are not used to it.**

And that is the journey I think we miss at most.

You see, when I played vanilla backed then, people casually joined BGs during their leveling experience, and since there were not much spells it slowly added up, and prepared those people by skill, but also that PvP is not that scary part of the game. Then they entered 60 lvl BGs and they were much more comfortable with making it main activity.

But, here I tried to make same thing. Well, you can probably guess how it went. 1-59 is full of twinks. So when someone non-twink want to incorporate PvP in daily routine, it's spawn -> death farm for dozens of minutes. Not good experience. And then, they reach 60 and they are not ready, what is happening there.

I think to support PvP, we should start on habits of people from lower levels, If those people will make a habit and get comfortable in BGs early on, they will more likely get into it on 60 and continue on.

Rewards, proper PvP gear, balance those are things that will only improve situation further on.

So what do I suggest? Well, not remove twinks from the game. They are fun and baby projects of those who have them. But I would suggest to make twinking more challenging. Before, creators of twinks couldn't cap their levels, so they had to strategize how many dungeons they can go and not every twink got to level which is ideal. Those which got lucky and planned well were something!

But here, as I noticed, twinking is pretty easy. You just need gold and 60 lvl alt on different account, or very patient friend. And just grind that dungeon 10000x times until you get there. Then, if it is easy, it makes BGs twink exlusive zones, and what happens on 60 lvl, well more skilled and proficient people in previous comments debate that.

I know that winks won't be removed from game, or now when jinn is out of the jar, it's hard to do something. But I would want to point out, that we should also focus on people still getting grasp on this game, and show them, that PvP might not be only one sided massacre.

Thank you!

## Post 127496 by Whoopie — 2025-04-19T13:19:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=127496#p127496 | page 7 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

After spending significant time in Turtle WoW PvP, it's clear that there are three fundamental issues holding the system back from being balanced, rewarding, and enjoyable:

 - Excessive burst damage
 - Lack of meaningful PvP progression and gear
 - Faction imbalance

Here’s how I propose we address each of these:

 **1. Burst damage**
Currently, burst damage in PvP is far too high. Players are often deleted in a single global cooldown, with little counterplay. While it might seem tempting to apply a flat -X% damage reduction in instanced PvP (BGs and Arenas), this solution comes with serious drawbacks:

 - It disproportionately benefits tank specs, making them even harder to kill.
 - It also enhances the value of healers, as healing becomes relatively more effective in longer fights.
 - In effect, it shifts the power imbalance, not fixes it.

The correct solution is ability-specific tuning. Each burst-heavy ability needs to be reviewed and adjusted on an individual basis and tuned down in PvP environments without affecting PvE. Yes, it’s more work, but it’s the only effective and sustainable fix that doesn’t create more problems elsewhere.

 **2. PvP Gear and Progression**
PvP currently lacks any meaningful reward structure. The grind is punishing, unrewarding, and ultimately not worth it. I propose a spec-specific PvP gear system with a reworked honor model that feels rewarding, and doesn’t require no-lifing to participate.

 **Honor System Changes:**
 - Players earn honor through all PvP activities: battleground wins/losses, honorable kills, and daily PvP quests.
 - Introduce a weekly honor cap (e.g., 50,000 honor per week) to limit burnout and prevent botting.
 - Create daily PvP quests to encourage consistent participation in both battlegrounds and world PvP.

 **PvP Gear Progression:**
Introduce three PvP gear tiers per spec — Green, Blue, and Epic — with gear stat allocation that respects each role (DPS, healer, tank) and offers PvP-specific survivability. All pieces would have lower damage values than PvE counterparts, but higher stamina and damage reduction from PvP sources, and lower + healing values. Tank specs would have no damage values on their items.

 **Green Gear**
 - Easily obtainable — players should be able to get a full set within ~20–30 hours of casual PvP play.
 - Allows newcomers to be competitive and not a dead weight in BGs.

 **Blue Gear**
 - Requires more honor and the green piece to upgrade.

 **Epic Gear**
 - Requires even more honor and the blue piece to upgrade.

Suggested Costs (numbers can be tuned as needed):
 - Green Item: 1,000 honor
 - Blue Upgrade: 5,000 honor + green item
 - Epic Upgrade: 15,000 honor + blue item

 **This system ensures:**
 - New players quickly become viable in PvP.
 - There’s always something to work toward without unreasonable time commitment.
 - PvP gear remains slightly weaker than PvE gear offensively but compensates through stamina and resilience-like damage reduction.

For example: Paladins would have to choose either Tank gear (with no STR) or Damage gear (with much lower stamina but with STR) or Healing gear (with medium stamina, +healing and no damage).

NUMBERS ARE ONLY REPRESENTATIVE TO MAKE A POINT!

GREEN
Paladin Tank chest:
+30 STA
+10 INT
+2% damage reduction from player sources

Paladin Damage chest:
+10 STA
+20 STR
+10 INT
+2% damage reduction from player sources

Paladin Healer chest:
+18 STA
+22 INT
+40 Healing
+2% damage reduction from player sources

BLUE
Paladin Tank chest:
+36 STA
+12 INT
+2% damage reduction from player sources

Paladin Damage chest:
+12 STA
+24 STR
+12 INT
+2% damage reduction from player sources

Paladin Healer chest:
+22 STA
+26 INT
+45 Healing
+2% damage reduction from player sources

EPIC:
Paladin Tank chest:
+40 STA
+15 INT
+2% damage reduction from player sources

Paladin Damage chest:
+14 STA
+27 STR
+14 INT
+2% damage reduction from player sources

Paladin Healer chest:
+25 STA
+30 INT
+50 Healing
+2% damage reduction from player sources

 **3. Faction Imbalance**
Queue times and team quality vary wildly between factions. This is a long-standing issue on Turtle. (To preserve immersion, players can retain their faction identity or be temporarily disguised in BGs, e.g., Horde players appear as Alliance).

The best fix is to implement cross-faction battlegrounds. Benefits include:
 - Shorter queues.
 - More evenly matched games.
 - Less faction-based frustration.

## Post 128514 by Zulnam (Bug Report Enthusiast) — 2025-04-25T22:27:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=128514#p128514 | page 7 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Frankly, when it comes to the gearing issue, I strongly believe retail has solved it a while ago and should be researched for fixing the gear issue.

In retail, when you join a battleground or arena, your item score, and as such your item stats, are increased to a default minimum.

This makes a great equal playing field for players. Not only that but the base pvp items you can get increase to a higher default item score, and are very easy to get. We're talking multiple items in a single day of playing; if you commit to it you could have all basic items in 2 days, 3 tops (if you keep losing).

Not only that but thanks to almost all gear having versatility and versatility being a primary stat for any class in pvp, this means you can get good gear from pvp without overshadowing pve.

Now, how to implement this in the vanilla client? No idea.

My suggestion would be to research how long it takes to get T1 on any class and make that the default time it takes to get the first PvP set (call it PvP T1).

Reason being T1 is a good baseline for an old server in PvP, and often easier to get than T0 (and now with the multi-spec set feature, more relevant as well).

 ---

That's one thing to keep PvP relevant. Second is to make daily victory quests that reward important regeants.

Look, ain't no one got the time to do all the damn grinding this game requires. And at this point, nobody on turtle wow joins BGs beyond the pvp enthusiasts and the truly masochists (and the ocassional alt trying to get the AV epics).

What we need is an incentive for non-pvpers to try the damn thing.

## Post 128531 by Halfgeek9 (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-04-26T01:56:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=128531#p128531 | page 7 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

A lot of great discussion here.

As someone who plays classic wow just for the pvp fun aspect, the fact that one needs to raid pve to progress in pvp is really a roadblock.

PvP itemization needs a major overhaul.

Resilience is needed on PvP gear to reduce chance of crit received, crit damage, and dot damage, to counter the power creep from higher PvE tier sets.

PvP gear needs to be good, but not BiS in PvE. Hence Resilience is the itemization stat sink, keeping PvE tier sets best for raid content, but inferior to PvP sets for actual PvP.

The effect of Resilience should give the PvP set a strong advantage in PvP.

This can be done by the dev team deciding what should be the target crit % & crit dmg modifiers in PvP that is acceptable without it being too bursty (it needs to remain bursty, just not too much). For example, if its 15% and 2x dmg crits, then make PvP itemization with crit % bonuses that is reduced by Resilience, so when both players fight each other in PvP gear, they don't end up with 25-30% crit rate.

## Post 128579 by Soulforged — 2025-04-26T12:21:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=128579#p128579 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Given that Turtle WoW is designed as a long-term project, not a seasonal server with a 1–2 year reset cycle, the power gap between veteran players and newcomers is inevitable and will only grow over time.

Because of this, it’s important to ensure that new or returning players, especially those who are primarily interested in PvP, can realistically compete and have fun without being forced into PvE grinds they might not enjoy.

In my opinion, a player who chooses to focus on PvP should be able to:

Progress meaningfully by playing PvP content alone.

Have a fair chance at high-end competitive play.

Enjoy the journey without feeling severely handicapped simply for not raiding.

To address this, I propose the following:

 1. Buff and Modernize Existing PvP Sets

Enhance the existing PvP gear (such as blue and epic rank sets) to be substantially more viable against top-tier PvE gear.

Make PvP gear the best choice for PvP content, without making it overpowered in PvE.

This creates a natural separation where PvE players dominate raids, and PvP players dominate battlegrounds and arenas.

 2. Make PvP Gear Easier to Obtain

Lower the barrier to acquiring basic PvP sets through accessible means such as:

PvP quests.

Honor marks.

Weekly participation rewards.

Allow players to start gearing quickly, and then gradually upgrade through more challenging PvP achievements like rated arenas or battleground milestones.

I think those are realictic, technically viable and can happen relatively fast without waiting 2 years.

Having said there are 2 issues:

High-end PvE players might feel devalued

Lower-end raids might die

I don't really know how to address those issues. Maybe we are looking to an absolute distinction of pve and pvp.

## Post 128902 by Neechy (Patch Note Conspiracy Theorist) — 2025-04-28T21:52:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=128902#p128902 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

How about a gearscore check built into the game, which allocates queued players only to BGs/arenas that have similarly geared players? It would be like weightclass rules in boxing - if a heavy fighter wants to get in the ring with a lightweight, he has to cut down so they are in the same weight class, and vice versa.

This could potentially eliminate the need for massive itemization overhauls and all the frustration that new/lower geared players have with getting pwned by BiS players. (Another alternative is of course stat squashing all tier levels, e.g. so power gap between MC and Kara40 is much less; something I wrote about in a previous post).

Basically, whatever items a player has equipped when they queue for BG/arena will lock them in the finder with similarly geared players (i.e. MC/BWL iLevels together, and Naxx/Kara40 iLevels together). If they change their equipment while in the queue, the finder will reallocate them based on their updated gearscore.

Once in a BG/arena there should be restrictions in place to how you can change your equipment, so that someone can't queue with low gear and then equip BiS gear once they are in.

There would obviously be some kinks to workout with this, but I see it as a way to ensure more fun and fair play.

>  Basically, whatever items a player has equipped when they queue for BG/arena will lock them in the finder with similarly geared players (i.e. MC/BWL iLevels together, and Naxx/Kara40 iLevels together). If they change their equipment while in the queue, the finder will reallocate them based on their updated gearscore.
>
>  Once in a BG/arena there should be restrictions in place to how you can change your equipment, so that someone can't queue with low gear and then equip BiS gear once they are in.
>
>  There would obviously be some kinks to workout with this, but I see it as a way to ensure more fun and fair play.

That would be dope. Would make a bwl twink and never leave that bracket lol.

## Post 131879 by Mitzter (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-05-19T08:51:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=131879#p131879 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Hello.

I'm a veteran WoW player of over 20 years, being a top PvP player for 2/3rd of my life, but  I am new to Turtle WoW. I can make anything, any weird spec, any class work in PvP, and I would like to add some feedback, theorycrafting and brainstorming when it comes to balancing PvP damage output.

First, I'll start with the possible solutions to balancing out damage output:
 1.  Resilience - this is the path that blizzard took, and  this would be a copy-paste of the TBC resilience.
 2.  Flat damage reduction in  PvP - the simplest solution, with the least amount of effort put in.
 3.  Custom stat - resilience lite, what was already experimented on with 3% less chance to be crit stat.
 4.  Health increase(including PvE) - simple solution in theory, very hard to implement.
 5.  Global damage output nerf(including PvE damage output) - same as the above  solution, simple but hard to implement, as it requires a lot of testing, rebalancing etc.

Now I'll get into the pros and cons  of each (most cons can also apply to pretty much any route that is chosen, but in a lesser or greater form):

 1.  Resilience

 **Pros:**
- The resilience stat ensures that PvP focused players have an advantage over PvE geared players. After all, why should a player that focuses on PvE and has never PvPd be on comparable power, or even stronger, than someone that PvPs 90% of their playtime.

- Resilience dampens the effect of the most oppressive forms of damage - those being dots and crits. Players geared in full T3 are able to completely erase someone with full duration of 2 dots. This feels incredibly bad on the receiving end. Dot classes if played correctly can properly stay at their max range of 36 yards, throw out 2 dots and gtfo.

- The amount of crit and bonus damage through attack power/sp people can reach  when fully geared is so high, that you can expect 1/3rd of the time to eat a 3k crit from a  spell and get completely deleted if  your healthpool is low enough. Some classes can even throw out instacasts that can easily reach 1-2k+ dmg. If your healthpool is 4k, that means two-three instacasts is all it takes for you to get deleted. This, of course, doesn't feel good on the receiving end.
 **Cons:**
- Resilience creates a problem with flexability. PvE geared players lose a lot of their power and end up feeling  like their efforts were for nothing getting all that gear through PvE. This in turn lowers participation in PvP, as then PvE players will feel obligated to grind out PvP gear in order to participate and compete in PvP  without getting 1shot. PvE participation would also get affected. Why would a PvP player jump into a raid if PvP gear is better than PvE gear for their chosen gamemode.

- Resilience requires you to maintain multiple gearsets. While this isn't anything new to some classes that can fulfill multiple roles, those same classes are going to have to maintain possibly double the  gearsets. Imagine having a bank full of healing, dps and  tanking gear for both game modes. It doesn't feel good having to run back to your bank and equip  your desired set for whichever gamemode. Minor inconvenience in the grand scheme of things, but still notable.
 2.  Flat damage  reduction  in PvP

This was tested in SoD, and I correctly predicted the shitstorm that it caused  before it was implemented.

 **Pros**

- Simple - damage is reduced globally, people aren't getting oneshot anymore.

- Minimal resources spent on developer time (literally 5 minutes)
 **Cons** (there's lots of them)

- Lowering damage globally doesn't fix the issue of which classes are overpowered and which ones are underpowered, and further increases the gap between those classes/specs. For example, if  before an offmeta class/spec relied on their setup into huge burst to kill someone, and that is no longer possible, without an additional way to create that opportunity, they simply lose.

- Lowering  PvP damage, instanced or globally, creates a damage discrepancy when doing PvE. It feels weird seeing different damage numbers against players  and mobs. Less experienced players would have no idea how much damage they would actually deal, what would be most optimal etc.

- Lowering PvP damage without  addressing mana costs can have dire implications for casters and  mana hungry specs. Some classes would simply be unable to finish a kill before their mana runs out. This never feels good. With the stage this server is in regarding gear tiers and class changes I don't believe this would be as big of a problem, but it would  mean that less geared players would be even more shafted and left unable to compete, as they would simply run out of mana trying to kill a fully geared player with 5k+ hp ontop of damage dampening. The beauty of vanilla/classic is being able to kill fully geared players with trash gear if  played correctly. Hell, I even killed fully geared lvl 60s as a lvl 50+ through outplays and superior game knowledge.

- Lowering PvP damage, if only applied to instances, doesn't fix the issue of people getting deleted in the open world, and further enhances damage discrepancy. It can also lead to two outcomes regarding the PvP population - either PvPers remain only in instanced BGs, like rats contained in a cage, which causes resentment towards the developers, or they decide to boycott instances and only remain in the open world if their class feels overly nerfed inside instanced BGs, which again, doesn't feel great for those players and creates further resentment.
 *There's a lot more cons with this  change, but I'll have to stop here to prevent a dissertation length  post.*

 3. Custom stat.

There isn't much to say about this, as most of the rules  that apply to resilience also apply to this, depending on how its implemented, but its a far more versatile option than simply introducing resilience, as it can be modified with other modifiers, such as reducing the effectiveness of cast time slowing effects, reducing the duration of CCs to prevent bad heartbeat RNG, increasing the effectiveness of vampirism, lowering the cost of spells etc.

 4. Health increase.

This change can take two routes - either a health increase through a new tier or through PvP gear, or a global health increase

 **Pros:**

- People have more health and are thus able to eat more damage before they die. Simple, once again, but only in theory.
 **Cons:**

- Trivializing PvE encounters if they are not adjusted properly.

- Players having a higher healthpool means they are now able to clear content solo that they weren't able to before, possibly upsetting the market balance and creating inflation. This would either warrant a  global mob/boss damage output buff, which also  makes lower tier raids harder to enter  for less geared players.

- Mana issues once again
(You  might as well work on a new expansion at this point)

 5. Global damage output nerf

 **Pros:**
- With reduced damage, people aren't getting oneshot! Great!!!!!1 Simple solution once again(kek  no)
 **Cons:**
- People would suddenly feel weaker, as if  they were all nerfed. Won't feel good for anyone to suddenly lose power overnight.

- Raids, dungeons and pretty much all outdoor mobs would need to be readjusted and  healthpools lowered if things need to at least "feel" the same as before, but with lower numbers on screen.

- Guess what, MANA ISSUES ofc.
 **Conclusion:**
The best solutions in my opinion would be a custom stat, similar to resilience in functionality, but more versatile and customizable, or simply adding resilience. **However**, The best approach would be to add the stat on MOST pieces  of gear, with PvP gear having a higher proportion of it. Taking the custom new stat that reduces crit  for example, it would look something like this:

PvE gear - 1% less chance to be crit by players.
PvP gear - 3% less chance to be crit by players.

This way PvE gear will still retain some relevance in PvP content.

Needless to say, PvP gear also needs  to be updated, with a  new "tier" added in in order to address its outdated performance, or to increase ease of access to PvP gear.  The current blue PvP and epic PvP sets can get some slight stat boosts(HWL/GM gear to be comparable in power to Naxx gear), with a new lower tier added in, which  is  easier to get (say, at around rank 5-6 for the  full set) that is comparable to stats with the current blue pvp set, and a slightly worse and/or different setbonus. Currently, by the time you reach the required rank to get the blue PvP set, you could already be near fully T2/T2.5 geared, which is comparable in power to the epic PvP set.

## Post 131920 by Mitzter (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-05-19T11:48:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=131920#p131920 | page 8 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **Ataika wrote: Mon Mar 31, 2025 11:12 am**
> Resilience vouching guys gonna realise it doesnt work with a huge damage disparcity we have between pve and pvp gear. Late wotlk patches had resilience system yet people still rolled over resilience guys with full pve penetration sets, the same will happen to our realm if pvp gear wont be buffed damage wise.

I am personally  against resilience gear, however it is the most cost effective solution with the least downsides if executed correctly.

While you are correct about wrath to an extent, running a lot of PvE pieces also meant you took far more damage in return. What wrath did well was making  sure both PvE and PvP gear  was viable for PvP. Some classes worked better with full PvP gear, some worked well with more PvE pieces that could afford it. It allowed for minmaxing. Most importantly - I never felt at a disadvantage fighting  PvE whales as a fully PvP  geared character, nor was it necessary for any class to run an abnormal amount of PvE gear in order to compete. A fully PvP geared player could use their survivability to their advantage, whereas the PvE geared players were more of a one trick pony in that regard, except some classes that like warlock that had soul link, mages that could make up for  being squishy with their mobility etc. Ideally in wrath you never wanted to drop lower than 450-600 resilience if you were  running PvE pieces or you'd get sneezed on and die. Even a frostmage with full wrathful gear, 0 haste and full spellpower/crit was viable and capable of obliterating anything, as was destro, rogues, warriors etc.

Ideally the best solution for an authentic vanilla experience is more health, armour and resistances added on to gear. But then a potential problem  arises of trivializing early PvE content in unforseen ways, and requires far more careful testing and planning, and how far the devs are willing to allow such trivialization.

## Post 131948 by scl83 — 2025-05-19T13:32:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=131948#p131948 | page 9 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Before we decide anything, we need to figure out what PVP should be:

A main activity you do at the end of the game (endgame).
Something extra you can do, like a profession.
Both of these things.
What made the original (Vanilla) version of the game so great was that you could get really good gear and then be powerful in all parts of the game: fighting monsters (PVE) and fighting other players (PVP). However, back then, it was rare to have the best (epic) gear compared to the more common blue and green gear. Now, most people have more epic gear than blue and green gear, which is a big change.

So, if we only think about players at the maximum level (level 60), since almost everyone at level 60 has similar quality gear these days, maybe it's time to try a different way of handling PVP.

I think PVP should now be its own main activity at the end of the game. There should be a clear difference between players who mostly fight monsters (PVE) and those who mostly fight other players (PVP). For example, PVP players shouldn't be able to use their gear effectively in PVE, and PVE players shouldn't be able to use their gear effectively in PVP without big downsides.

My problem is that people usually only talk about PVP from the point of view of dealing damage (DPS). We also need to think a lot about healing and tanking.

Tanks in PVP should be able to take a lot of damage but not deal much damage themselves. Healers should be able to heal a lot but maybe not do much damage.

Everyone should be able to enjoy PVP, not just players who deal damage. The game developers (Blizz) made a big mistake here by mostly focusing on damage dealers, which led to not enough healers.

I think we should have two new PVP stats: Hardiness and Recovery.
(I don't like using the same names as Blizzard in WoW, we don't need to!)

 **Hardiness** - Makes you take less damage.
 **Recovery** - Makes you receive more healing.

I believe gear is the most important thing for PVP, not temporary boosts or anything else.

You should be able to get PVP sets for Tanks, Healers and DPS

Tier 0.5 PVP gear - craftable, green quality
Tier 1 PVP gear - Blue quality
Tier 2 PVP gear - Epic quality
Tier 3 PVP gear - Epic quality

Here are some examples:

PVP cloth belt (for damage)
Intellect: 14
Stamina: 22
Hardiness: Damage reduction from players by 12
Recovery: Healing received from players by 10
Increases damage and healing against players by 30

PVP cloth belt (for healing)
Intellect: 18
Stamina: 20
Hardiness: Damage reduction from players by 8
Recovery: Healing received from players by 10
Increases your healing done by 16

So, PVP players should have a lot of health, hit hard with their regular attacks against other players, and be harder to kill with more stamina and damage reduction from Hardiness. Healers should also benefit a lot by having their healing reduced outside of PVP, but players with PVP gear will have increased Recovery, so they receive more healing in PVP combat.

I do believe healers in PVP should get more intellect to increase their mana pool.. I think it is stupid to go oom after about 5 heals! You get much less time to drink in a battle situation, so increasing the healing time but giving more mana would be beneficial to everyone and increase the fun. I also believe healers should be weaker, but not too drastically weaker.

I think Tanks don't really need a lot more stamina that other players, but tank gear could have more Hardiness.

This would mean PVP players would be good at PVP but not very good at PVE, and the other way around.

However, I'm not sure what can or should be done for players below level 60. I'm not even sure if that's important to think about.

## Post 132920 by Drakereinz — 2025-05-25T11:44:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=132920#p132920 | page 9 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

> **scl83 wrote: Mon May 19, 2025 1:32 pm**
> Before we decide anything, we need to figure out what PVP should be:
>
>  A main activity you do at the end of the game (endgame).
>  Something extra you can do, like a profession.
>  Both of these things.
>  What made the original (Vanilla) version of the game so great was that you could get really good gear and then be powerful in all parts of the game: fighting monsters (PVE) and fighting other players (PVP). However, back then, it was rare to have the best (epic) gear compared to the more common blue and green gear. Now, most people have more epic gear than blue and green gear, which is a big change.
>
>  So, if we only think about players at the maximum level (level 60), since almost everyone at level 60 has similar quality gear these days, maybe it's time to try a different way of handling PVP.
>
>  I think PVP should now be its own main activity at the end of the game. There should be a clear difference between players who mostly fight monsters (PVE) and those who mostly fight other players (PVP). For example, PVP players shouldn't be able to use their gear effectively in PVE, and PVE players shouldn't be able to use their gear effectively in PVP without big downsides.
>
>  My problem is that people usually only talk about PVP from the point of view of dealing damage (DPS). We also need to think a lot about healing and tanking.
>
>  Tanks in PVP should be able to take a lot of damage but not deal much damage themselves. Healers should be able to heal a lot but maybe not do much damage.
>
>  Everyone should be able to enjoy PVP, not just players who deal damage. The game developers (Blizz) made a big mistake here by mostly focusing on damage dealers, which led to not enough healers.
>
>  I think we should have two new PVP stats: Hardiness and Recovery.
>  (I don't like using the same names as Blizzard in WoW, we don't need to!)
>
>  **Hardiness** - Makes you take less damage.
>  **Recovery** - Makes you receive more healing.
>
>  I believe gear is the most important thing for PVP, not temporary boosts or anything else.
>
>  You should be able to get PVP sets for Tanks, Healers and DPS
>
>  Tier 0.5 PVP gear - craftable, green quality
>  Tier 1 PVP gear - Blue quality
>  Tier 2 PVP gear - Epic quality
>  Tier 3 PVP gear - Epic quality
>
>  Here are some examples:
>
>  PVP cloth belt (for damage)
>  Intellect: 14
>  Stamina: 22
>  Hardiness: Damage reduction from players by 12
>  Recovery: Healing received from players by 10
>  Increases damage and healing against players by 30
>
>  PVP cloth belt (for healing)
>  Intellect: 18
>  Stamina: 20
>  Hardiness: Damage reduction from players by 8
>  Recovery: Healing received from players by 10
>  Increases your healing done by 16
>
>  So, PVP players should have a lot of health, hit hard with their regular attacks against other players, and be harder to kill with more stamina and damage reduction from Hardiness. Healers should also benefit a lot by having their healing reduced outside of PVP, but players with PVP gear will have increased Recovery, so they receive more healing in PVP combat.
>
>  I do believe healers in PVP should get more intellect to increase their mana pool.. I think it is stupid to go oom after about 5 heals! You get much less time to drink in a battle situation, so increasing the healing time but giving more mana would be beneficial to everyone and increase the fun. I also believe healers should be weaker, but not too drastically weaker.
>
>  I think Tanks don't really need a lot more stamina that other players, but tank gear could have more Hardiness.
>
>  This would mean PVP players would be good at PVP but not very good at PVE, and the other way around.
>
>  However, I'm not sure what can or should be done for players below level 60. I'm not even sure if that's important to think about.

I agree with separating the 2 gameplay types (PvE vs. PvP). I would just like to add that as far as balancing is concerned, a bear, prot warrior/pally, or enhance shaman (since they're tanks now) should require 2 players to kill them, but they shouldn't be able to kill a player on their own. The same is true for a healer. It should require 2 players to take one down.

That's the only way I can see the holy trinity serving a purpose in PvP. This is all assuming equal gear and skill.

> **Forumdweller wrote: Fri May 23, 2025 2:43 pm**
> > **Konstantin19 wrote: Fri May 23, 2025 10:24 am**
> > i like the idea of toning down the general damage!
> >  for an example if we drop all overall damage and healing lets say -15%
> >
> >  devs can adjust this to -15% hp tp all mobs,that will keep pve intact
> >  pvp damage will be less bursty
>
>   what about specs that rely on burst i.e destro lock?
>
>  if your 1-2-3 combo fails to kill some1 you pretty much lost the duel right then and there. nerfing overall damage dealt even if that comes at an equal reduction of healing done is an enormous change to the pvp meta and leaves open other questions like: what about absorbs? do priest bubbles also get reduced? if they do -15% to absorbs that is a change that is way less disruptive compared to how a -15% nerf is gonna affect glass cannon specs/classes in particular. warrior and yet again hybrids are the ones who benefit from these blanket changes
>
>  i want the game to be less bursty but i think pvp needs a complete rework and its not as simple as just reducing damage dealt (unless they specifically tweak damage numbers on each individual ability). its gonna change the game alot

It's a group oriented game, and you can coordinate damage spikes.

 1v1 insta gibbing IS the problem, and not being able to do so IS the point here.

Simply put, good PvP doesn't have 100-0's in 1v1s. That's just called hitting a PvE rotation, and winning due to bad balance.

Good PvP involves coordinated damage spike windows, proper CC/rupt usage, peeling/ranging, timer/resource management... and just enough RNG to keep the conclusion less than 100% predictable to keep it fresh/fun and require situational adaptation. Which ultimately is where skill expression comes into play.

It's should not about hitting buttons in a row and getting the result you want 100% of the time, it should be about making choices that increase the probability of a positive result in the current situation more often than the other guy.

Hitting 1..2..3.. is none of that.

## Post 134926 by Vermz — 2025-06-09T20:20:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=134926#p134926 | page 10 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

I don’t think that gear or mechanical adjustments designed to artificially prolong fights—or further separate PvE and PvP, such as introducing resilience—are a good idea. They would alienate the server from its essence, which is defined as a Vanilla+ experience and should remain true to that.

Why would someone raid if the gear they earn functions differently in battlegrounds than gear earned through PvP - potentially devalueing the raid gear? For me, it’s the combination of both elements that makes the game enjoyable: raiding to get good gear and then PvPing with it—an essential part of the Vanilla WoW experience. Such changes could further fragment the already small PvP community, potentially driving more players away from PvP content rather than attracting a broader audience.

I believe the developers should refrain from more global changes that affect multiple classes or core game mechanics—like global CC resistance or resilience. Instead, targeted class-specific tuning would allow a more cautious and measured approach, making it easier to monitor and balance outcomes.

If changes are considered for PvP, Paladins might be a good starting point, as their high player count suggests they’re outperforming other classes. Their strong defensive toolkit combined with high burst damage can be overwhelming and has satirical proportions right now. This might also apply to Shamans I think. And Survival Hunters in both PvP and PvE.

PvP has gotten so bad that people are starting to think ANY change at all might help. I guess it's my fault for not engaging on the forums more vehemently. I'll yell into the void one more time.

I'm concerned that, while some of the changes you’ve proposed might sound good on paper, we should expect even more problems to arise for each one you try to fix. Instead of throwing the kitchen sink at burst damage, I’d prefer that you aim for a higher level of class balance—so that each class has a fair chance to defeat any other class when similarly geared.

Rather than creating new maps and announcing new features, I’d prefer you focus on fixing what's already in the game. For example, please revert the Alterac Valley map layout to how it was before. The current iteration of AV is NOT GOOD. I won’t go into massive detail here about the problems caused by turtle-exclusive changes.

Please walk back these ideas and start with something simpler, like removing all the invisible walls added to Alterac Valley. These invisible walls are not only misguided, but they also fail to achieve what you intended.

Misguided changes—like the ones I'm mentioning and some of those announced here—continually put player retention and the health of the PvP community at risk. Frankly, I have good reason to doubt that the risks you’re taking will be handled properly.

## Post 135219 by Jhaerik (Barrens Chat Casualty) — 2025-06-11T02:31:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=135219#p135219 | page 10 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Be better off just having a single server, making Warmode the actual "PvP" server, and just drop the flagging system.

Seperate Warmode and non-Warmode just like they do Hardcore and non-Hardcore. Fixes a lot of problems. Queue times. World PvP. Janky ass blue-wall ganking. Etc.

Also don't let Warmode people group cross faction. At all.  Let blue name folks just be the civis. Frankly I'd love to have something closer to the original experience without cross faction grouping/raiding, and would prefer a "Warmode" guild. Personally my biggest gripe with Turtles PvE experience is the whole silly cross faction stuff.

Allow Warmode to be turn ON once, and back OFF once. Remove flagging entirely.

No reason to divide the population across two servers. Right now PvP sucks on the PvE server, and PvE sucks on the PvP server. Why not just have the best of both worlds?

I feel like the devs struggle a bit when it comes to what types of PvP are fun, and how to handle it. Which to be fair, so did the original vanilla devs. I personally think the above ideas would just make for an all around better experience. People that want PvP could do it somewhere where finding groups for alts isn't awful, and the overall quality of world PvP, and wait times of BGs would go down.

Also fixes the absolutely asinine restriction of "you can't pvp while in a cross faction group/raid" rule... which Turtle GMs can neither enforce, nor can the players follow.

## Post 136024 by Grinspoon — 2025-06-17T09:35:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=136024#p136024 | page 11 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

A few suggestions for a PVP rework:

 - do not focus on arena. it would require extensive rebalancing of classes to work well. I don't see that happening. objective based pvp is a lot more forgiving.

 - keep the rank grind long but remove all decay and allow it to be more of an on/off marathon than a continuous sprint. let players chip away at it over months/years with breaks in-between if they don't have the time to grind 24/7. in the spirit of the project -> slow and steady. (punish afkers harshly to avoid abuse)

 - put the resi on pvp gear and make the first set a bit easier/faster to obtain. a naxx geared player should still want to keep their gear on over blue pvp gear but they'd have less of an advantage over a fresh pvp player. a global buff wouldn't help with the gigantic gear disparity.

## Post 136441 by pillowed — 2025-06-20T18:32:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=136441#p136441 | page 11 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

This is real simple.

The turtle wow team fell into the same "power creep" trap/cycle that Retail fell into.

The cycle goes something like this:
Q: How do we keep players engaged?
A: Let's add new dungeons and raids.
Q: What incentive is there for players to do these new dungeons and raids?
A: New shiny gear that is better than the current gear.
 **A FEW MOMENTS LATER**
Q: Why has pvp become so imbalanced and terrible?
A: New shiny gear too powerful. **In this case, new shiny gear + talent "optimization" combined to create a deadly combo of unexpected problems in pvp**
Q: How do we fix it?
A: The same kind of crap that retail wow tried.....

The first and primary issue is that they needed to change the talents or add new gear in two different phases to understand if there would be unintended consequences with either gearing or talent "optimization" before proceeding to the next phase.

The second issue is that they were not creative enough in coming up with a new way to incentivize players to play these new dungeons. Another, maybe not better, way to incentivize players would have been to take a more "Albion Online" approach and make the new content more focused on crafting materials or any number of other ideas that don't create such a power spike.

Now that we are here in a Turtle Wow- Retail moment... They really only have two options. Either embrace that there are actually two different games; PVP and PVE. Create better PVP gear that should be better for pvp than raid gear. And yes, that does include pvp stats like resiliance. I understand the reluctance to add stats like resiliance but resilance itself wasn't the problem. The problem in retail wow was that it took so long to get both. It is completely possible to balance pvp and pve gear where neither is terribly out of whack with the other based on approximate progression progress. At the same time, making the pvp gear wearer having a slight advantage in pvp while the pve gear wearer has a slight advantage in pve.

But i type all of this to say that the one thing we know isn't going to work is a flat percentage reduction across the board. And worst yet, only in BG's. They may as well not even implement that and try to actaully fix the problem as I understand the original post.

## Post 136489 by Dyco — 2025-06-21T04:40:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=136489#p136489 | page 11 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

My suggestions are:

 1. Rank system as sod or so: No rank decay, less time dependent system

 2. Itemization rework for PvP rank sets: currently these sets works or give benefits only to specific specs.

 3. Cross faction bgs: aiming to end premade bgs and faction domination in certain bgs.

 5. PvP class balance: there are some classes, spec and spells that are way to op in PvP. Pallys for example, or spell pushback caused by arcane mages.

 6. Early access to minor PvP sets: for example some crafted items which works as beginner equipment for a beginner PvP player.

 7. Less engineering dependency for PvP: add other professions some useful items at PvP too.

## Post 136493 by Notsomany — 2025-06-21T07:28:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=136493#p136493 | page 11 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

In addition to what you have written, you can also focus on the outdoor PvP mechanism to improve the PvP system. Daily quests can be placed in historical areas (contested territories like ashenvale, arathi etc.), events can be organized to obtain buffs in these areas and these events can provide buffs to the continent NPCs and players. On the other hand, usable PvP items (such as catapults, glaive throwers, flying mounts that depend on time and “speed” to reach high peaks) can be added to contested territories and battlegrounds. This will allow for more effective and fun use of battlefields. If there are elements to trigger such items in the battlegrounds, they should be set more optimally so that both a 40 year old player and a 7 year old player are willing and able to trigger them.

Happy Hunting all.
smiling_turtle

## Post 140033 by Jackthechanger — 2025-07-17T06:25:00Z
<!-- url: https://forum.turtlecraft.gg/viewtopic.php?p=140033#p140033 | page 11 | era: pre-1.18.1 -->

Hello!
I have been the ganker, and the gankee in multiple occasions. I enjoyed the times when I got the surprise, or out smarted a target, and will even admit to a few unsavory afk kills, but I also loathe with it happens to me afk otherwise.
For the most part it sucks when you are at a disadvantage, when you have no reason to engage but still come out losing.
Lots of suggestions on this post and I'm sorry if i post an idea that was already in the thread, but my suggestion focuses around a debuff, nothing major, can't be dispell like (res sick) have to wait it out. Now the debuff proc requires a smart AI to trigger/ or a simple but solid code that targets players who kill honorless targets. It could be lowering stats, attk power, stamina, or honor gained for the next ___ mins.
Honorless Targets: now this buff is triggered when you spawn in from various transportation, or hearth back to a location. What if your by entering a contested zone, based off nearby players (Stealthed or otherwise) it procs the buff onto the player.
Now this tells the player two things: there are enemies, and they are Higher lvl or my lvl. BUT nothing else. For the Ganker, it could also proc: lvl 45 rogue vs lvl 45 war. Both could be honorless IF a lvl 60 was in the zone (contested zone) when the players entered. The buff needs a duration so you must move, be smart and be aware. Nothing game breaking, nothing that strips the power form a fully capped lvl 60, just a lil debuff(recommend 10-20% dmg debuff or equipment durability penalty)
